(105)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=&\frac{q_{1}^{\perp}X_{2}X_{3}^{3/2}\bar{z}_{3}\bar{z}_{2}^{3}\bar{z}_{1}^{2}z_{2}}{\langle 45\rangle\langle 56\rangle\Omega_{\langle 4|q_{1}^{\perp}|-]}\Omega_{\langle-|P_{456}|+]}r_{456-}}\\
&+\frac{[46][56]^{2}z_{3}z_{2}z_{1}(1-\bar{z}_{3}){}^{2}(1-\bar{z}_{2}){}^{2}(1-\bar{z}_{1}){}^{2}}{[45]s_{456}\Omega_{\langle-|P_{56}|4]}\Omega_{\langle+|P_{45}|6]}\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp}\sqrt{X_{3}}\sqrt{X_{2}}}\\
&+\frac{X_{2}X_{3}\bar{z}_{2}^{4}\bar{z}_{1}^{4}z_{3}z_{1}(1-z_{2}){}^{3}(1-z_{1}){}^{2}}{\langle 45\rangle\Omega_{\langle+|P_{45}|6]}\Omega_{\langle+|{q_{4}^{\perp}}|6]}\Omega_{\langle 5|P_{+4}P_{+456}|-\rangle}r_{+456}r_{+45}}\\
&-\frac{\sqrt{X_{3}}\big(\bar{z}_{3}-(1-\bar{z}_{2})\big){}^{3}\bar{z}_{1}^{3}z_{3}z_{2}(1-\bar{z}_{3})(1-\bar{z}_{2})(1-\bar{z}_{1})(1-z_{1}){}^{2}}{\langle 56\rangle\Omega_{\langle-|P_{56}|4]}\Omega_{\langle-|P_{456}|+]}\Omega_{\langle 5|P_{+4}P_{+456}|-\rangle}\Omega_{\langle+|P_{56{q_{4}^{\perp}}}P_{56}|-\rangle}\sqrt{X_{2}}}\\
&-\frac{q_{1}^{\perp}\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp}r_{56-}^{2}\bar{z}_{3}\bar{z}_{2}\bar{z}_{1}^{2}z_{1}(1-\bar{z}_{1}){}^{2}(1-z_{3})(1-z_{2})}{\langle 56\rangle s_{q_{1}4}\Omega_{\langle 4|q_{1}^{\perp}|-]}\Omega_{\langle 5|P_{q_{1}^{\perp}4}\text{}|-]}\Omega_{\langle+|P_{56{q_{4}^{\perp}}}P_{56}|-\rangle}X_{2}^{3/2}X_{3}^{3/2}}\\
&-\frac{q_{1}^{\perp}\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp}\bar{z}_{3}\bar{z}_{2}\bar{z}_{1}z_{1}(1-\bar{z}_{3})(1-\bar{z}_{2})(1-\bar{z}_{1})(1-z_{3}){}^{2}(1-z_{2}){}^{3}}{\langle 45\rangle s_{6q_{4}}\Omega_{\langle+|{q_{4}^{\perp}}|6]}\Omega_{\langle 5|P_{q_{1}^{\perp}4}\text{}|-]}X_{2}X_{3}^{2}z_{2}^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(106)

Utilising these primitive CEVs, we now systematically demonstrate how colour-dressed three-parton-emission CEVs involving a quark-antiquark pair can be constructed from colour-ordered CEVs using the relations in sections
5.5.1
and
5.2
.

Helicity configuration
\nu_{4}=\oplus,\nu_{5}=\ominus,\nu_{6}=\oplus

Here we present the colour-dressed CEV
\mathcal{C}_{3}^{gq\bar{q}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
. Beyond the primitive colour-ordered CEV
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
required for Eq. (
80
), five additional CEVs must be incorporated. We now explicitly demonstrate how these additional CEVs relate to the primitive CEVs listed above through momentum permutation,
reversal and photon decoupling identities.
The second colour-ordered CEV appearing in Eq. (
80
), namely
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
,
is related to the primitive
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
by the reversal identity followed by the momentum permutation
{\mathbb{P}_{654}}
,

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{654}.}

(107)

To represent the third term in Eq. (
80
), we first apply the photon decoupling identity, and then determine the relevant colour-ordered partials in terms of the primitive ones using the permutations in section
5.5.1
.
Applying the photon decoupling identity we have:

\displaystyle\hskip-15.0ptC_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big).

(108)

Here we recognise the first term as one of our primitive CEVs, Eq. (
103
), and we determine the second and third ones from the primitives by applying momentum permutations:

\displaystyle C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{546}},

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{564}.}

(109)

To express the fourth term in Eq. (
80
),

C_{3}\bigl(\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr),

one must first apply the reversal identity, and subsequently apply the same process as above, i.e., use the photon decoupling identity and subsequently relate the CEVs to the primitive ones using momentum permutations.
Thus, in the first two steps we have:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-2.0ptC_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&=-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,.\end{split}

(110)

The three CEVs we obtained here are related to the primitives by the following momentum permutations:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{465}}\\
&C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{4}^{\oplus},g_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{645}}\\
&C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{654}}.\end{aligned}

(111)

The fifth term in Eq. (
80
) is obtained simply by applying a momentum permutation to the primitive CEV in Eq. (
106
):

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{564}}.

(112)

The final term in Eq. (
80
) is obtained by applying the reversal identity and then performing a momentum permutation

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{465}}.

(113)

Thus, we have successfully expressed all the colour-ordered CEVs in MSLCV which are required for the dressed CEV,

\mathcal{C}^{gq\bar{q}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},q_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
,
according to Eq. (
80
).

Helicity configuration
\nu_{4}=\oplus,\nu_{5}=\oplus,\nu_{6}=\ominus

We now wish to obtain the colour-dressed CEV for the helicity configuration
\nu_{4}=\oplus,\nu_{5}=\oplus,\nu_{6}=\ominus
. In this case Eq. (
80
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-10.0pt\mathcal{C}_{3}^{g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\\
&g^{3}\left[\big(T^{c_{4}}T^{c_{1}}T^{d_{4}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{4}}T^{d_{4}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\right.\\
&\hskip 10.0pt+\big(T^{c_{4}}T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{4}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&\hskip 10.0pt\left.+\big(T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{4}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{4}}\big)_{i_{6}\bar{\imath}_{5}}C_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\right]\,.\end{split}

(114)

The configuration containing negative-helicity quarks paired with positive-helicity anti-quarks can be mapped to positive-helicity quarks with negative-helicity anti-quarks through
charge-conjugation symmetry:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3}^{g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\mathcal{C}_{3}^{g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big).

(115)

This yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-10.0pt\mathcal{C}_{3}^{g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\\
&g^{3}\left[\big(T^{c_{4}}T^{c_{1}}T^{d_{4}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{4}}T^{d_{4}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\right.\\
&\hskip 10.0pt+\big(T^{c_{4}}T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{4}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&\hskip 10.0pt\left.+\big(T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{4}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)+\big(T^{d_{4}}T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{4}}\big)_{\bar{\imath}_{6}i_{5}}C_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\right]\,,\end{split}

(116)

where we used Eq. (
50
) for each of the colour-ordered CEVs.

Next we wish to express each of the colour-ordered CEVs appearing in Eq. (
116
) in terms of the primitive ones in Eqs. (
101
) through (
106
). The first CEV in Eq. (
116
),
C\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),
is just the primitive of Eq. (
101
). The second CEV in Eq. (
116
) may be obtained by first applying the reversal identity, and then using a momentum permutation of Eq. (
106
):

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{654}.}

(117)

The third CEV in Eq. (
116
) is obtained by first applying the photon decoupling identity, and then identifying the resulting colour-ordered CEVs as primitive ones after appropriate momentum permutations. The photon decoupling identity yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=&-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&-C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,,\end{split}

(118)

where the last two terms are obtained from the primitives by applying momentum permutations

\displaystyle C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},{q}_{4}^{\oplus},g_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{546}}\,,

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},{q}_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{564}}.

(119)

In the fourth CEV in Eq. (
116
)
we first apply the
reversal identity and then proceed in a similar fashion to the previous case.
We obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-20.0ptC_{3}\big(q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
=&-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,,\end{split}

(120)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},{q}_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{465}}\\
&C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},{q}_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{645}}\\
&C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},{q}_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big|_{\mathbb{P}_{654}}.\end{aligned}

(121)

To obtain the fifth CEV in Eq. (
116
),
we directly apply momentum permutation to the primitive in Eq. (
104
)

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},{q}_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{564}}.

(122)

Finally, to determine the sixth CEV in Eq. (
116
) we first apply the reversal identity and then identify the resulting colour-ordered CEV as a momentum permutation of Eq. (
103
):

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\ominus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\bigg|_{\mathbb{P}_{465}}.

(123)

The remaining helicity configurations, containing two negative and one positive helicities may be obtained from the results above by complex conjugation.
Having presented the complete set of colour-dressed CEVs for triple emissions
containing a single
q\bar{q}
pair, we
now proceed to outline the higher-multiplicity MREVs.

6
Multi-Regge Emission Vertices (MREV): a Mathematica library

In the previous section, we presented explicit expressions for CEVs with multiplicity up to three. The results are rather complex rational functions even in terms of the shorthand notation we adopted here.
For higher multiplicities, it is necessary to use computer algebra to obtain and present the results. To this end, we have prepared a Mathematica library,
Multi-Regge-Emission-Vertices
(
MREV
)
yuyu_2017_github
,
which contains results for MREVs with any helicity configuration and up to multiplicity four.
Figures
14
and
15
categorize our PEV and CEV results according to multiplicity; those shown in light blue are new results. The library files containing the corresponding data are listed in Table
2
.

The implementation consists of two layers. The lower layer is a library of
{\cal N}=4
sYM colour-ordered MREVs of gluons and gluinos, which we derived based on amplitudes computed using GGT package
Dixon:2010ik
by taking the appropriate high-energy limits, and tabulated in several data files. The upper layer is a map between these sYM objects and QCD ones implementing the relations discussed in section
5.1
. This map is only non-trivial starting with MREVs with two distinct quark-antiquark pairs.

We note that the
MREV
library is fully self-contained: all the MREVs to multiplicity four have already been explicitly extracted and stored. Nevertheless, we present here also the extraction step, so as to make it possible to obtain results for MREVs at higher multiplicities or different (massless) theories from the corresponding amplitudes.

We proceed as follows. First, in subsection
6.1
, we present an explicit example demonstrating the extraction of both MREVs from a given amplitude with massless partons, thereby illustrating how expressions in our library have been derived.
Subsequently, in subsections
6.2
–
6.4
, we explain the use of the library by considering MREVs at multiplicity four. Additional examples involving PEVs with lower multiplicities are provided in Appendix
D
.

Figure 14:
Peripheral Emission Vertices (PEVs) corresponding to final state multiplicities of up to four partons collected in our
Multi-Regge-Emission-Vertices
(MREV) library
yuyu_2017_github
. The diagrams labelled
(a)
,
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
respectively represent final states with 1, 2, 3, or 4 partons, including cases with pure gluons as well as configurations containing up to two quark–antiquark pairs.

Figure 15:
Central-emission vertices (CEVs) corresponding to final state multiplicities of up to four partons collected in our
Multi-Regge-Emission-Vertices
(MREV) library
yuyu_2017_github
. The diagrams labelled
(a)
,
(b)
,
(c)
, and
(d)
respectively represent final states with 1, 2, 3, or 4 partons, including all flavour configurations, ranging from pure gluons to two quark–antiquark pairs.

6.1
Examples of PEV and CEV extraction

In this section we provide a quick tutorial on using the
Multi-Regge-Emission-Vertices
(
MREV
)
yuyu_2017_github
package and library. We demonstrate both how we used the
GGT

Dixon:2010ik
package to derive the MREVs and how to retrieve the resulting expressions from the library.
We use two examples. One is the QCD colour-ordered PEV,

P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
,
and the other is the QCD colour-ordered CEV

C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
.
To determine these MREVs we start, respectively, with the following
colour-ordered seven-point amplitudes:

\displaystyle A_{2\to 5}^{\mathrm{QCD}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)

\displaystyle=A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,3}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big),

(124)

\displaystyle A_{2\to 5}^{\mathrm{QCD}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)

\displaystyle=A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big).

(125)

Note that we have chosen these
amplitudes to be helicity conserving in the relevant high-energy limits which define the
P_{1\to 4}
and
C_{3}
MREVs under consideration. That is, gluons 1 and 7 have opposite helicities, as do gluons 2 and 3.
Also note that in Eq. (
124
) we use gluino pairs of the same R-charge to replace both quark-antiquark pairs, rather than the naïve replacement,

A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,2}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)
, which features
gluinos of two distinct R-charge indices.
In this way we eliminate the scalar interaction, and thus directly obtain the required QCD amplitude, in line with section
5.1
(see Eq. (
44
)).
In contrast, for
(
125
) there is a single gluino pair so there are no scalar interactions. Thus, here no subtleties arise and the colour-ordered amplitudes with a straightforward replacement of quarks by gluinos are identical in the two theories.

To begin we need to load the
GGT

Dixon:2010ik
and
S&M

Maitre:2007jq
packages:

⬇

SetDirectory
[
"path/to/GGT"
];

Next, we extract the PEV and CEV by rewriting the amplitudes in terms of the minimal set of light-cone variables (MSLCV),

\begin{split}A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,3}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)\to A_{2\to 5}^{\prime}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)\\
A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)\to A_{2\to 5}^{\prime\prime}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)\end{split}

(126)

by leveraging the spinor-to-momentum transformation, Eq. (
201
), and the mapping to MSLCV, Eq. (
10
). This is implemented in
MREV
via:

⬇

Amp1

=

GGTnmhv4fermS
[7,

{3,

4},

{2,

5}]

//

Expand

//

Map
[
GGTtoMSLCV
[#,

7]

&];

Amp2

=

GGTnmhv2ferm
[7,

3,

4,

5]

//

Expand

//

Map
[
GGTtoMSLCV
[#,

7]

&];

where, as explained in Appendix C of
GGT

Dixon:2010ik
, the function
GGTnmhv4fermS[7, {3, 4}, {2, 5}]
generates the
\mathcal{N}=4\,\mathrm{sYM}
helicity amplitude for a 7-parton process in which particles 3 and 4 are positive-helicity gluinos, particles 2 and 5 are negative-helicity gluinos, and the remaining particles are gluons, with gluon 7 having negative helicity, while the rest (gluons 1 and 6) have positive helicity. For the second case,
the function
GGTnmhv2ferm[7, 3, 4, 5]
generates the one in which particle 4 is a positive-helicity gluino, particle 5 a negative-helicity gluino, and particles 3 and 7 are negative-helicity gluons, while the rest, gluons 1 and 6 have positive helicities.
The function
GGTtoMSLCV
directly converts helicity amplitudes from the
GGT
package into the MSLCV representation.

The PEV,
P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
, is extracted from the amplitude (
124
) according to the following equation

\begin{split}&\hskip-20.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\,\simeq\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,y_{5}\,\simeq\,y_{6}\gg y_{7}}A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,3}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)\\
&\qquad=s\,\,P_{1\to 4}\big(\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,3}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\frac{1}{t_{4}}{P}_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{7}^{\ominus},\,g_{1}^{\oplus}\big)\\
&\qquad=s\,\,P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\frac{1}{t_{4}}{P}_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{7}^{\ominus},\,g_{1}^{\oplus}\big),\end{split}

(127)

where we have used eq. (
44
) in the final line.
In MSLCV it is

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{X_{4}\to\infty}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{2\to 5}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)

(128)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle sP_{1\to 4}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)\frac{1}{t_{4}}P_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(q_{n-3}^{\perp}\big).

This extraction is automated using the
getPEV
function as follows:

⬇

PEV$

=

Amp1

//

Map
[
getPEV
[#,

7,

{
p
,

m
}]

&];.

Here,
{p, m}
specifies the helicities of the gluons
g_{1}
and
g_{7}
, respectively, which are needed to obtain the correct
P_{1\to 1}
in Eq. (
127
).

For amplitudes with a different multiplicity, the argument
7
, which denotes the seven-point amplitude
A_{2\rightarrow 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\mathrm{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\ominus},\tilde{g}_{A,3}^{\oplus},\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)
, should, of course, be replaced with the appropriate number of external legs.

The CEV
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
is extracted directly from the amplitude, Eq. (
125
), as follows:

\begin{split}&\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\,y_{5}\simeq\,y_{6}\gg\,y_{7}}A_{2\to 5}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\text{sYM}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,4}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)\\
&\hskip 40.0pt=\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\,y_{5}\simeq\,y_{6}\gg\,y_{7}}A_{2\to 5}^{\text{QCD}}\big(g_{1}^{\oplus},\,g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus}\big)\\
&\hskip 40.0pt=s\,{P}_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{4}}\,{P}_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{7}^{\ominus},\,g_{1}^{\oplus}\big)\,.\end{split}

(129)

In MSLCV, it reads

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{X_{1},X_{4}\to\infty}

\displaystyle{A}_{2\to 5}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\big)=

(130)

\displaystyle sP_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(q_{1}^{\perp}\big)\frac{1}{t_{1}}C_{3}\big(q_{1}^{\perp},X_{2},X_{3},{z}_{1},{z}_{2},{z}_{3}\big)\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}P_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(q_{n-3}^{\perp}\big)\,.

This extraction is performed automatically by the
getCEV
function as:

⬇

CEV$

=

Amp2

//

[
getCEV
[#,

7,

{
p
,

p
,

m
,

m
}]

&];.

Here,
{p, p, m, m}
specifies the helicities of the gluons
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
,
g_{3}
and
g_{7}
, respectively, which are needed to obtain the correct
P_{1\rightarrow 1}
in Eq. (
129
).
This extraction
reproduces the result given in Eq. (
104
). Furthermore, one can verify that the extracted expressions
cev
and
pev
, for a given helicity configuration, are independent of the amplitude from which they are obtained.

As illustrated in this example, the input for the MSLCV representation of positive rapidity PEVs takes the form

pev[helicity labels][MSLCV labels]
,
and that of CEVs takes the form

cev[helicity labels][MSLCV labels]
.
The dependence on the MSLCV is specified according to Eq. (
) and Eq. (
), respectively.

As explained above, because of the additional scalar interaction

P_{1\rightarrow 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},Q_{\sigma_{3}}^{\oplus},q_{\sigma_{4}}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{\sigma_{5}}^{\ominus},g_{\sigma_{6}}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\neq P_{1\rightarrow 4}^{{\mathcal{N}=4\,\mathrm{sYM}}}\big(\tilde{g}_{Q,2}^{\ominus},\tilde{g}_{Q,3}^{\oplus},\tilde{g}_{q,4}^{\oplus},\tilde{g}_{q,5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,.

(131)

The command
pev
(
cev
) directly provides the QCD PEV (CEV)
after applying the map from
{\cal N}=4
to QCD, which eliminates the scalar interactions.
If one is interested instead in the
{\cal N}=4
sYM prior to applying this map, one may use instead
PEV
(
CEV
) defined in capital letters.
The following table provides the complete set of commands used to retrieve MREVs from the library and manipulate the obtained expressions.

Next we note that
P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
and
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
are related by the factorization property in the limit where the rapidity
y_{3}
is taken much larger than the others:

\begin{split}&\hskip-40.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\,\gg\,y_{4}\simeq\,y_{5}\simeq\,y_{6}}P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&=s\,{P}_{1\rightarrow 1}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,Q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),\end{split}

(132)

which provides a second consistency check of the results.

).

As explained in section
5
,
momentum permutations are useful for obtaining the complete set of colour-ordered MREVs required for colour-dressing.
To implement such permutations it is convenient to use a momentum representation of the MREV expressions.
Starting from the MSLCV representation, it is straightforward to convert to the momentum representation by inverting Eq. (
10
). This is implemented by the functions
pevtopevp
and
cevtocevp
, which convert the MSLCV representation of
pev
and
cev
to momentum representation
pevp
and
cevp
, respectively, for up to four final-state particles:

⬇

pev
[{
qM
,

Q
},

{
qP
,

Q
},

{
qP
,

q
},

{
qM
,

q
},

{
P
,

g
}]

//

pevtopevp

cev
[
qP
,

qM
,

P
]

//

cevtocevp

For the PEV under consideration,
P_{1\rightarrow 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},Q_{3}^{\oplus},q_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
, all permutations of the emitted particle momenta with fixed helicity and particle content can then be called by

\displaystyle P_{1\rightarrow 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{2}^{\ominus},Q_{\sigma_{3}}^{\oplus},q_{\sigma_{4}}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{\sigma_{5}}^{\ominus},g_{\sigma_{6}}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qP,q\},\{qM,q\},\{P,g\}][\text{$\sigma$}_{3},\text{$\sigma$}_{4},\text{$\sigma$}_{5},\text{$\sigma$}_{6}]}.

(133)

Here, we omit the label “2” (corresponding to the incoming parton) in
[\sigma_{3},\,\sigma_{4},\,\sigma_{5},\,\sigma_{6}]
. The momentum dependence of
g_{2}^{\oplus}
and
g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}
does not need to be specified in the code, since it is fixed by momentum conservation in the plus and transverse directions, respectively.

Similarly, for the CEV under consideration,
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{\sigma_{4}}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{\sigma_{5}}^{\ominus},\,g_{\sigma_{6}}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
, all permutations of the emitted particle momenta
with fixed helicity and particle content, can be called by

\displaystyle C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{\sigma_{4}}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{\sigma_{5}}^{\ominus},\,g_{\sigma_{6}}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\mathtt{cevp[qP,\,qM,\,P][1,\text{$\sigma$}_{4},\text{$\sigma$}_{5},\text{$\sigma$}_{6}]}.

(134)

Here, the label “1” denotes the off-shell gluon
g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}
in the colour-ordered CEV (
cf.
Figure
9
). The momentum dependence of the other off-shell gluon
g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}
in Eq. (
134
) does not need to be specified in the code. Further details on how to use the library will be provided in the next subsections in the context of the more complex multiplicity-four MREVs.

6.2
The
g^{*}g\to gggg
PEV and the
g^{*}g^{*}\to gggg
CEV

According to Eq. (
32
), the four-gluon PEV
g^{*}g\rightarrow gggg
presented first in
DelDuca:1999iql
takes the following form

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{2}^{h_{2}},\ g_{3}^{h_{3}},\ g_{4}^{h_{4}},\ g_{5}^{h_{5}},\ g_{6}^{h_{6}},\ g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=g^{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{4}}\big(F^{a_{\sigma_{2}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{3}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}\big)_{c_{1}c_{4}}P_{1\to 4}\big(g_{{\sigma_{2}}}^{h_{\sigma_{2}}},\ g_{{\sigma_{3}}}^{h_{\sigma_{3}}},\ g_{{\sigma_{4}}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\ g_{{\sigma_{5}}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\ g_{{\sigma_{6}}}^{h_{\sigma_{6}}},\ g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,.

(135)

From this formula it is clear that one needs to compute different permutations of the colour ordered PEV. This may be done by constructing the corresponding momentum permutation replacement list in the MSLCVs, as described in section
5.5.1
. However, within the code, it is more convenient to first convert the expression into the momentum representation and then directly permute the momentum labels before returning to the MSLCVs. In the following we explain how this is done using in the library.

To get the data from the library, one first issues the following commands to initialise the
MREV
package:

⬇

Quit
[];

SetDirectory
[
"path/to/MREVinit.m"
];

Import
[
"MREVinit.m"
];.

The next step is to load the four-gluon PEVs
g^{*}g\rightarrow gggg
expressed in terms of MSLCVs, which are tabulated in
PEV_4_emissions_purely_gluon.m
:

⬇

Import
[
"PEV_1to4_ggggg.m"
];.

Next, one should initialise the momentum representation of the colour-ordered PEVs:

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevtopevp[\#]\&/@}\big\{\mathtt{pev[P,P,P,M,M],}\,\mathtt{pev[P,P,M,M,P],}\,\mathtt{pev[P,M,P,M,P],}\,...\big\},

where
P
and
M
represent
g^{\oplus}
and
g^{\ominus}
, respectively, and the set in the curly brackets consists of all helicity configurations that are required in order to write the colour dressed PEV of interest.
Then a given colour-ordered pure gluon PEV in the momentum representation can be called by

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{{2}}^{h_{2}},\,g_{{\sigma_{3}}}^{h_{\sigma_{3}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{4}}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{5}}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{6}}}^{h_{\sigma_{6}}},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\mathtt{pevp[h_{2},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{3}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{4}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{5}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{6}}][\text{$\sigma$}_{3},\text{$\sigma$}_{4},\text{$\sigma$}_{5},\text{$\sigma$}_{6}]}

(136)

with
\mathtt{h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{i}}=P\text{ or }M}
. For example, one instance is given by

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{{2}}^{\oplus},\,g_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{4}^{\ominus},\,g_{{3}}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\mathtt{pevp[P,M,P,M,P][5,6,4,3]}.

(137)

In a similar manner we now consider the colour-dressed four-gluon CEV. According to Eq. (
31
) it has the following form
DelDuca:1999iql

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{4}^{h_{4}},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}},\,g_{7}^{h_{7}},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=g^{4}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{4}}\big(F^{a_{\sigma_{4}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{5}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{6}}}F^{a_{\sigma_{7}}}\big)_{c_{1}c_{2}}C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{{\sigma_{4}}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{5}}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{6}}}^{h_{\sigma_{6}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{7}}}^{h_{\sigma_{7}}},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big).

(138)

As with the PEV case, to begin one loads the
four-gluon CEVs
g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow gggg
in terms of MSLCVs, which are
tabulated in
CEV_4_gggg.m
:

⬇

Import
[
"CEV_4_gggg.m"
].

Next, one should initialise the momentum representation of the colour-ordered CEVs

\displaystyle\mathtt{cevtocevp[\#]\&/@}\big\{\mathtt{cev[P,P,M,M],}\,\mathtt{cev[P,M,M,P],}\,\mathtt{cev[M,P,M,P],}\,\mathtt{cev[P,M,P,M],}\,...\big\}

where
P, M
in the arguments represent
g^{\oplus}
and
g^{\ominus}
respectively.
Then a given colour-ordered pure-gluon CEV in the momentum representation can be called by

\displaystyle{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{{\sigma_{4}}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{5}}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{6}}}^{h_{\sigma_{6}}},\,g_{{\sigma_{7}}}^{h_{\sigma_{7}}},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\mathtt{cevp[h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{4}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{5}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{6}},h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{7}}][1,\text{$\sigma$}_{4},\text{$\sigma$}_{5},\text{$\sigma$}_{6},\text{$\sigma$}_{7}]}

(139)

with
\mathtt{h_{\text{$\sigma$}_{i}}=P\text{ or }M}
and where “1” denotes the off-shell gluon
g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}
in the colour-ordered CEV (cf. Figure
9
). The momentum dependence of the other off-shell gluon
g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}
does not need to be specified in the code because of momentum conservation. For example, one permutation is given by

\displaystyle{C}_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{7}^{\oplus},\,g_{4}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)=\mathtt{cevp[M,P,M,P][1,6,7,4,5]}.

(140)

In the following subsections we present multiplicity four MREVs with quarks.

6.3
The
g^{*}\bar{q}\to qggg
PEV and the
g^{*}g^{*}\to\bar{q}qgg
CEV

In analogy with the pure-gluon case discussed so far, the
MREV
library contains the full set of MREVs with quarks. These MREVs have not been computed before, and are presented here for the first time.

The complete set of MREVs with gluons and quarks is presented in Figures
14
and
15
. Table
2
summarises these, pointing to the relevant files in the library. In the present subsection and in the following one we will discuss the structure of the colour-dressed MREVs containing respectively one and two quark-antiquark pairs, and show how to use the library to determine the relevant ingredients.

For PEVs of the type
g^{*}\bar{q}\to qggg
, according to Eq. (
33
) we consider the colour-dressing example of
{\cal P}_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
:

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\big(T^{a_{4}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{4}}\big)_{i_{3}{\bar{\imath}}_{2}}{P}_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{4}}T^{a_{5}}T^{c_{4}}T^{a_{6}}\big)_{i_{3}{\bar{\imath}}_{2}}{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{4}}T^{c_{4}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{6}}\big)_{i_{3}{\bar{\imath}}_{2}}{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{4}}T^{a_{4}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{6}}\big)_{i_{3}{\bar{\imath}}_{2}}{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\cdots,

(141)

where the ellipsis stand for additional colour orderings, where
g_{4}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{\oplus}
and
g_{6}^{\oplus}
are permuted in the argument of
{P}_{1\to 4}
in conjunction with the corresponding colour indices
a_{4},\,a_{5}
and
a_{6}
.
Next we show how the individual colour-ordered PEVs are obtained. One first loads the data in terms of MSLCVs,

⬇

Import
[
"PEV_1to4_qqbggg.m"
],

and then initialise the momentum representation as

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevtopevp[\#]\&/@}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\big\{\mathtt{pev[qM,qP,M,P,P]},&\mathtt{pev[qM,qP,M,P,Pc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{pev[qM,qP,M,Pc,Pc]},&\mathtt{pev[qM,qP,Pc,Pc,Mc]}\big\},\end{array}\,.

Here,
qP
and
qM
represent
q^{\oplus}
and
\bar{q}^{\ominus}
, respectively, while
Pc
and
Mc
denote
g^{\oplus}
and
g^{\ominus}
, respectively; the additional letter
c
indicates that their colour position is after the off-shell gluon
g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}
, following the clockwise ordering and starting from the incoming parton 2 (
cf.
Figure
9
).
The colour-ordered PEVs in Eq. (
141
) can be obtained as follows:

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[qM,qP,M,P,P][3,4,5,6]}

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[qM,qP,M,P,Pc][3,4,5,6]}

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[qM,qP,M,Pc,Pc][3,4,6,5]}

\displaystyle{P}_{1\to 4}\big(g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},q_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[qM,qP,Pc,Pc,Mc][3,6,5,4]}.

(142)

We now consider a CEV example. According to Eq. (
34
),
the colour-dressed CEV,
\mathcal{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
, may be expressed as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=\,\,

\displaystyle\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{5}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{5}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}{C}_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}{C}_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{5}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}{C}_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{5}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{\bar{q}_{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}T^{c_{1}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{5}}T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{5}}T^{c_{1}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big(T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{7}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\,\left[g_{6}^{\ominus}\leftrightarrow g_{7}^{\oplus},\,a_{6}\leftrightarrow a_{7}\right].

(143)

Next one loads the CEVs by

⬇

Import
[
"CEV_4_qqbgg.m"
],

and subsequently initialises the momentum representation using

\displaystyle\mathtt{cevtocevp[\#]\&/@}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\big\{\mathtt{cev[qM,qP,M,P]},&\mathtt{cev[Pc,Mc,qPc,qMc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[qM,qP,M,Pc]},&\mathtt{cev[P,Mc,qPc,qMc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[M,P,qPc,qMc]},&\mathtt{cev[qM,qP,Pc,Mc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[P,qM,qP,Mc]},&\mathtt{cev[M,qPc,qMc,Pc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[P,qM,qP,M]},&\mathtt{cev[Mc,qPc,qMc,Pc]},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[M,P,qM,qP]},&\mathtt{cev[qPc,qMc,Pc,Mc]}\big\}\,.\end{array}

Finally, one can get the colour-ordered CEVs in Eq. (
143
) using

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[qM,qP,M,P][1,4,5,6,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[Pc,Mc,qPc,qMc][1,7,6,5,4]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[qM,qP,M,Pc][1,4,5,6,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[P,Mc,qPc,qMc][1,7,6,5,4]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[M,P,qPc,qMc][1,6,7,5,4]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[qM,\,qP,\,Pc,\,Mc][1,\,4,\,5,\,7,\,6]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[P,\,qM,\,qP,\,Mc][1,\,7,\,4,\,5,\,6]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[M,\,qPc,\,qMc,\,Pc][1,\,6,\,5,\,4,\,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[P,\,qM,\,qP,\,M][1,\,7,\,4,\,5,\,6]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[Mc,qPc,qMc,Pc][1,6,5,4,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[M,P,qM,qP][1,6,7,4,5]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[qPc,qMc,Pc,Mc][1,5,4,7,6]}.

(144)

6.4
The
g^{*}\bar{q}\to q\bar{Q}Qg
PEV and the
g^{*}g^{*}\to\bar{q}q\bar{Q}Q
CEV

Here we consider MREVs with two quark-antiquark pairs. We consider the general case where these two pairs have different flavours which we denote respectively by
q
(
\bar{q}
) and
Q
(
\bar{Q}
).
Using
\mathcal{P}_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
as an example, the colour dressing of the PEV
g^{*}\bar{q}\to q\bar{Q}Qg
is as follows,

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt\mathcal{P}_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{(T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{6}})_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}\delta_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}P_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+(T^{c_{5}}T^{a_{6}})_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}\delta_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+T^{c_{5}}_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}T^{a_{6}}_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\left[\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus}\leftrightarrow\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\;\bar{\imath}_{2}\leftrightarrow\bar{\jmath}_{4}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\,\,+\biggl\{(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{5}})_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}\delta_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+(T^{a_{6}}T^{c_{5}})_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}\delta_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+T^{a_{6}}_{j_{5}\,\bar{\imath}_{2}}T^{c_{5}}_{i_{3}\,\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\left[\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus}\leftrightarrow\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\;\bar{\imath}_{2}\leftrightarrow\bar{\jmath}_{4}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad-\delta_{qQ}\left[Q_{5}^{\oplus}\leftrightarrow q_{3}^{\oplus},\;j_{5}\leftrightarrow j_{3}\right],

(145)

where
\delta_{qQ}
equals
1
if the flavours are identical, or
0
otherwise. We first load the PEVs, written in MSLCVs, using:

⬇

Import
[
"PEV_1to4_qqbQQbg_.m"
],

and then initialise the momentum representation of the PEV in Eq. (
145
)
by

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevtopevp[\#]\ \&/@}

\displaystyle\big\{

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\}]}

\displaystyle\mathtt{pev[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\}]}

\displaystyle\mathtt{pev[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{M,\,g\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\}]}

\displaystyle\mathtt{pev[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{M,\,g\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\}]}

\displaystyle\mathtt{pev[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\},\ \{M,\,g\}]}

\displaystyle\mathtt{pev[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\}]}\big\}.

Here, we include additional arguments,
q
,
Q
,
g
, (or
gc
), to label the particle content as a quark of flavour 1, a quark of flavour 2, and a gluon, respectively.
Then, the colour-ordered PEVs appearing in Eq. (
145
) in the momentum representation are given by

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\}][3,\,5,\,4,\,6]}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\}][3,\,5,\,4,\,6]}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{M,\,g\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\}][3,\,6,\,4,\,5]}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{M,\,g\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\}][3,\,6,\,5,\,4]}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{qM,\,Q\},\ \{qP,\,Q\},\ \{M,\,g\}][3,\,4,\,5,\,6]}

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to 4}\big(\bar{Q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,Q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{6}^{\ominus},\,\bar{q}_{2}^{\ominus},\,q_{3}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

\displaystyle\hskip 11.99998pt=

\displaystyle\,\,\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,\,q\},\ \{qP,\,q\},\ \{Mc,\,gc\},\ \{qPc,\,Q\},\ \{qMc,\,Q\}][3,\,6,\,5,\,4]}.

The colour-dressed CEV
g^{*}g^{*}\to\bar{q}q\bar{Q}Q
is as follows,

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\mathcal{C}_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{(T^{c_{5}}T^{c_{1}})_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}\delta_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+(T^{c_{5}}T^{c_{1}})_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}\delta_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+T^{c_{5}}_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}T^{c_{1}}_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\left[\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus}\leftrightarrow\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\;\bar{\imath}_{4}\leftrightarrow\bar{\jmath}_{6}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\,+\biggl\{(T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{5}})_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}\delta_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}C_{4}\big(\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+(T^{c_{1}}T^{c_{5}})_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}\delta_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+T^{c_{1}}_{{j_{7}}{\bar{\imath}_{4}}}T^{c_{5}}_{{i_{5}}{\bar{\jmath}_{6}}}C_{4}\big(\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},Q_{7}^{\oplus},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\left[\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus}\leftrightarrow\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\;\bar{\imath}_{4}\leftrightarrow\bar{\jmath}_{6}\right]\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad-\delta_{qQ}\left[Q_{7}^{\oplus}\leftrightarrow q_{5}^{\oplus},\;j_{7}\leftrightarrow j_{5}\right],

where the
\delta
-function in the final line adds the additional terms which arise with identical quark flavours. To use the library, we begin as usual by loading the CEV data in terms of MSLCV,

⬇

Import
[
"CEV_4_qqbQQb_.m"
],

and initialising the momentum representation by

\displaystyle\mathtt{cevtocevp[\#]\&/@}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\big\{\mathtt{cev[\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\}],}&\mathtt{cev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}],}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}],}&\mathtt{cev[\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}],}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathtt{cev[\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\},\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\}],}&\mathtt{cev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}]}\big\}.\end{array}\,

The colour-ordered CEVs appearing in (
) in the momentum representation are given by

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\}][1,6,7,4,5]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}][1,4,5,6,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}][1,5,4,6,7]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}][1,5,4,7,6]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\},\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,q\}][1,7,6,5,4]},

\displaystyle C_{4}\big(\bar{Q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,Q_{7}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}_{{4}}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{cevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}][1,4,5,7,6]}.

(147)

7
Relations among MREVs

Many relations between colour-ordered amplitudes are known, including the photon decoupling (dual Ward) identity
Bern:1990ux
;
Mangano:1990by
;
Mangano:1987xk
, the Kleiss-Kuijf relation
Kleiss:1988ne
, the reversal (reflection) identity
Bern:1994fz
and the
\mathcal{N}=1
SUSY Ward identity
Grisaru:1977px
. In this section, we study whether and how these
translate into
relations among colour-ordered MREVs.
Although some of these identities have been investigated for pure-gluon MREVs in
DelDuca:1999iql
, here we extend them to general parton flavours. In particular, we build upon ref.
DelDuca:1999iql
by including the on-shell and off-shell photon decoupling identities for MREVs, as well as supersymmetric Ward identities at higher multiplicities. Together, these relations provide a robust consistency check of the results. These checks are complementary to the factorization properties of MREVs in the MRK, soft, and collinear limits, which we study in Appendix
B
.

Usually these relations apply independently of the specific theory considered.
Notably, there is one exception regarding the photon decoupling identity, that when colour-ordered amplitudes or MREVs involve two distinct quark flavours, the identity does not hold within QCD, but it is restored upon including additional contributions from
\mathcal{N}=4
super Yang-Mills (sYM) theory. This will be demonstrated in section
7.1
.

7.1
Photon Decoupling Identity for MREVs

Colour-ordered amplitudes satisfy photon decoupling identities
Bern:1990ux
;
Mangano:1990by
;
Mangano:1987xk
. Let us first recall how these arise and then see how they apply in the context of MREVs.

Consider first a
U(N_{c})
gauge theory. A tree-level
n
-point amplitude of gauge bosons can be expressed in terms of the gauge-group generators in a trace basis as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2\to n-2}(\{p_{i},\varepsilon_{i},a_{i}\})

(148)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{n-2}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{S}_{n}/\mathrm{Z}_{n}}\operatorname{Tr}\big(T^{a_{\sigma(1)}}\ldots T^{a_{\sigma(n)}}\big)\times A_{2\to n-2}\big(p_{\sigma(1)},\varepsilon_{\sigma(1)}\,;\,\ldots\,;\,p_{\sigma(n)},\varepsilon_{\sigma(n)}\big)\,,

where the triplet
(p_{i},\varepsilon_{i},a_{i})
represents the momentum, polarization vector and colour index of parton
i
, respectively.
This
U(N_{c})
theory can be interpreted as a theory of
N_{c}^{2}-1
“gluons” of a
SU(N_{c})
non-Abelian gauge group and a single “photon” of an Abelian
U(1)
gauge group. Since the
U(1)
generator has vanishing commutators with the
SU(N_{c})
ones, there is no interaction between the
U(1)
photon and the
SU(N_{c})
gluons. Then, any amplitudes involving one photon must vanish, i.e., upon singling out parton
1
as the photon,

\mathcal{A}_{2\to n-2}\left(\left\{p_{i},\varepsilon_{i},a_{i}\right\}_{i=2}^{n};p_{1},\varepsilon_{1},a_{1}\right)=0\,.

(149)

Since the
U(1)
generator is proportional to the identity and the traces

\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(T^{a_{\sigma(2)}}\cdots T^{a_{\sigma(n)}}\bigr)

form an independent basis, one may look at just one given trace. In particular, by extracting the coefficient of

\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(T^{a_{2}}\cdots T^{a_{n}}\bigr)
—where the
T^{a_{U(1)}}
is inserted in each possible slot in
\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(T^{a_{2}}\cdots T^{a_{n}}\bigr)
—Eq. (
149
) implies a decoupling identity for the colour-ordered amplitude:

\displaystyle A_{2\to n-2}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varepsilon_{1}};\,p_{2},\varepsilon_{2};\,p_{3},\varepsilon_{3};\,\ldots;\,p_{n},\varepsilon_{n}\bigr)+\,A_{2\to n-2}\bigl(p_{2},\varepsilon_{2};\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varepsilon_{1}};\,p_{3},\varepsilon_{3};\,\ldots;\,p_{n},\varepsilon_{n}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\,\cdots+\,A_{2\to n-2}\bigl(p_{2},\varepsilon_{2};\,p_{3},\varepsilon_{3};\,\ldots;\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varepsilon_{1}};\,p_{n},\varepsilon_{n}\bigr)=\,0\,,

(150)

where we highlight the photon in blue. Eq. (
150
) can be interprested as cyclically permuting the photon around the colour-ordered amplitude. The colour-ordered amplitudes in Eq. (
150
) are identical to amplitudes of
n
gluons in
SU(N_{c})
gauge theory, so this establishes a relation among pure gluon amplitudes.
Using the cyclicity property reviewed in section
5.2
, one can also rewrite it as

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in Z_{n-1}}A_{2\to n-2}\big(g_{1}^{h_{1}},g_{\sigma_{2}}^{h_{\sigma_{2}}},\ldots,g_{\sigma_{n}}^{h_{\sigma_{n}}}\big)=0,

(151)

where
Z_{n-1}
represents cyclic permutations.

This conclusion naturally extends to
\mathcal{N}=4
sYM amplitudes with gluons and gluinos, as both particles are in the adjoint representation of the gauge group, so their colour factors can be represented using the trace basis as in Eq. (
148
). However, due to the fermionic nature of gluinos,
anticommuting them amounts to a minus sign,
and hence the equivalent of Eq. (
151
) for amplitudes involving gluinos has a minus sign for terms corresponding to odd permutations of gluinos compared to those having an even permutation (see e.g.
Luo:2005my
).
Note that this odd or even nature is determined regardless of how many gluons are interleaved, or how the gluons are ordered.
For example, considering six-point colour-ordered amplitudes of two pairs of gluinos and two gluons, one may formulate the following relation:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\,A_{2\to 4}(\tilde{g}_{A,1}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\ominus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tilde{g}_{B,4}^{\oplus}}},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}})-A_{2\to 4}(\tilde{g}_{A,1}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tilde{g}_{B,4}^{\oplus}},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}})

(152)

\displaystyle+A_{2\to 4}(\tilde{g}_{A,1}^{\ominus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tilde{g}_{B,4}^{\oplus}},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}})+A_{2\to 4}(\tilde{g}_{A,1}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tilde{g}_{B,4}^{\oplus}})

\displaystyle+A_{2\to 4}(\tilde{g}_{A,1}^{\ominus},\,\tilde{g}_{A,2}^{\oplus},\,\tilde{g}_{B,3}^{\ominus},\,g_{5}^{h_{5}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tilde{g}_{B,4}^{\oplus}},\,g_{6}^{h_{6}})\,.

Based on the discussion in Ref.
Dixon:2010ik
and in section
5.1
above, QCD colour-ordered amplitudes involving gluons and quarks of a single flavour share identical photon decoupling identities with the corresponding
\mathcal{{N}}=4
sYM amplitudes, where the quarks are traded for gluinos. However, for amplitudes with two or more distinct quark flavours, some photon decoupling identities do not hold in QCD; instead, they intertwine
\mathcal{N}=4
sYM amplitudes with QCD ones. Next, we will discuss how these decoupling identities extend to PEVs and CEVs.

7.1.1
Permuting on-shell partons

Figure 16:
Starting from the photon decoupling identity for particular “mother” amplitudes, one can derive the photon decoupling identities for colour-ordered MREVs by considering the high-energy limit where
X_{1},X_{n-3}\to\infty
.
The set of diagrams in the upper row contribute to the MREV photon-decoupling identity, while those in the lower row exhibit power suppression, as the corresponding colour-ordered amplitudes have physical poles scaling as
\sim{1}/{X_{1}}
or
\sim{1}/{X_{n-3}}
when
X_{1},X_{n-3}\to\infty
.

As shown in Figure
16
, by taking the high-energy limit (see Eq. (
24
)) of the photon decoupling identities of colour-ordered amplitudes as in Eqs. (
151
), we can extract the photon decoupling identities for MREVs.
The figure describes the case of
(n-4)
emission CEVs, derived from
n
-point mother amplitudes in the limit
X_{1},X_{n-3}\to\infty
. In this
configuration, the particles labelled 2 and 3, which we take to be helicity- and flavour- conserving, represent the
target
, while the particles
labelled 1 and
n
denote the
projectile
(these also conserve helicity and flavour). By permuting gluon 4 through all admissible positions, distinct colour-ordered CEVs are generated. However, no CEV is generated by placing
gluon 4
between particles 2 and 3 or between 1 and
n
, as these configurations correspond to subleading contributions in the high-energy limit taken here.

We have already seen the photon decoupling identity for pure gluon CEVs in (
51
). We now explore a few examples of these identities for MREVs containing one or two quark pairs. First, consider the colour-ordered CEV
g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow gq\bar{q}
where we obtain the following photon decoupling identity:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})+C_{3}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})+C_{3}^{\text{}}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q^{\oplus}_{5},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})

(153)

\displaystyle+C_{3}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}),

where we cyclically inserted
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}}
in all relevant positions.
Note that the third CEV in Eq. (
153
), where the quark and antiquark are interleaved by a gluon, does not feature directly in a colour-dressed CEV (
80
) in QCD.
These relations are nonetheless useful in expressing such colour-ordered objects in terms of others, as we have done for example in eq. (
110
) above.
In a similar way, considering the colour-ordered PEV
qg^{*}\to\bar{q}q\bar{q}g
we get:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1\to 4}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},q^{\oplus}_{2},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}(q^{\oplus}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})

(154)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt+P_{1\to 4}(q^{\oplus}_{2},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}(q^{\oplus}_{2},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3},q^{\oplus}_{5},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt+P_{1\to 4}(q^{\oplus}_{2},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3},q^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{6},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{\oplus}_{4}},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}).

Also here the second and fourth term do not feature in the colour-dressed PEV in QCD.
The codes for checking Eqs. (
153
) and (
154
) are given in Appendix
E.4
.

Next, upon translating an on-shell quark around the colour-ordered amplitude, we obtain the following identity amongst the colour-ordered CEVs for
g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow q\bar{q}g
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=C_{3}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)-\,{C_{3}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}\,

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-{C_{3}^{\text{}}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}+\,C_{3}^{\text{}}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr),

(155)

where the signs are determined based on the relative ordering of the quarks alone.

Consider now colour-ordered CEVs involving two quark-antiquark pairs of the same flavour,
g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow q\bar{q}q\bar{q}
. By moving the
q_{4}^{\oplus}
around one obtains the following identity:

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\ C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)-\,{C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}

\displaystyle+\,{C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,q^{\oplus}_{6},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}-\,C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\,C_{4}^{\text{}}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)\,.

(156)

Moving on to similar CEVs for two
distinct
flavours,

g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow q\bar{q}Q\bar{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\ C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,Q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)-\,{C_{4}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,Q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}

\displaystyle+\,{C_{4}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\mathrm{sYM}}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,Q^{\oplus}_{6},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)}-\,C_{4}^{\text{}}\bigl(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,Q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\,C_{4}^{\text{}}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q^{\oplus}_{4}},g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},\,Q^{\oplus}_{6},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{7},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr),

(157)

where we note that the signs remain the same as in Eq. (
7.1.1
). In contrast to Eq. (
7.1.1
) however, Eq. (
7.1.1
) is not an identity within QCD: recall that in Section
5.1
(see Eq. (
40
) there), we saw that the CEV
C_{4}^{\mathrm{QCD}}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,Q_{6}^{\oplus},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{Q}_{7}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
vanishes. In turn, in
\mathcal{N}=4
sYM, where the corresponding CEV is non-vanishing thanks to the scalar interaction,
there is such an identity. Since all but one of the terms in this identity match QCD CEVs, Eq. (
7.1.1
), can be read as one that mixes CEVs of the two theories.
Similarly, for the PEV
\bar{q}g^{*}\rightarrow\bar{q}q\bar{q}g
, by moving the
\bar{q}_{3}^{\ominus}
around we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1\to 4}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)-P_{1\to 4}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},q^{\oplus}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

(158)

\displaystyle+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle-P_{1\to 4}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},q^{\oplus}_{2},q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr),

while with distinct flavours,
\bar{q}g^{*}\rightarrow\bar{q}Q\bar{Q}g
, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1\to 4}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},Q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)-P_{1\to 4}^{\mathcal{N}=4\,\mathrm{sYM}}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},Q^{\oplus}_{4},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

(159)

\displaystyle+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},Q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{5},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)+P_{1\to 4}^{\text{}}\bigl(q^{\oplus}_{2},Q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle-P_{1\to 4}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{3}},q^{\oplus}_{2},Q^{\oplus}_{4},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{5},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)\,,

where here again there is no corresponding identity strictly within QCD.
These examples, along with several others, are provided in the accompanying Mathematica notebook
Examples_v0.nb
.
We have explicitly verified the photon decoupling identities for all MREVs of up to four final-state partons. Next we move on to discuss the extension of the photon decoupling identities to off-shell gluons.

7.1.2
Permuting off-shell gluons

Following the argument presented at the beginning of the section, the decoupling identity can be seen as a redundancy in expressing the amplitudes in the trace basis, regardless of whether the particles are on-shell or off-shell. Therefore, the off-shell gluons
g^{*}_{{q}_{1}^{\perp}}
or
g^{*}_{q_{n+1}^{\perp}}
can also play the role of a “decoupling photon”.

For CEVs, permuting
g_{{q}_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}
gives rise to:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{n-4}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,p_{\sigma_{4}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,p_{\sigma_{5}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,\ldots,\,p_{\sigma_{n-1}}^{h_{\sigma_{n-1}}},\,g^{*}_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}\big)+C_{n-4}\big(p_{\sigma_{4}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,p_{\sigma_{5}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,\ldots,\,p_{\sigma_{n-1}}^{h_{\sigma_{n-1}}},\,g^{*}_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}\big)

(160)

\displaystyle+\ldots+\,C_{n-4}\big(p_{\sigma_{4}}^{h_{\sigma_{4}}},\,p_{\sigma_{5}}^{h_{\sigma_{5}}},\,\ldots,\,p_{\sigma_{n-1}}^{h_{\sigma_{n-1}}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,g^{*}_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}\big).

Thus, the permutation of the off-shell gluon
g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}
in the CEV is handled in the same way as an on-shell gluon, with no additional sign introduced.
For example, for the colour-ordered CEV process
g^{*}g^{*}\to g\,\bar{q}\,q
, as in Eq. (
160
), permuting
g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}
we get

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\ C_{3}\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)+C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)

\displaystyle+C_{3}^{\text{}}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)+C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}}},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big).

(161)

We may straightforwardly generalise these relations to higher-multiplicity CEVs. For example, let us consider the colour-ordered CEV for
g^{*}g^{*}\to x\,q\,\bar{q}\,y
, with
x
and
y
being either gluons (
x=g_{4}
,
y=g_{7}
), quarks of the same flavour (
x=q_{4}
,
y=\bar{q}_{7}
) or quarks of a different flavour (
x=Q_{4}
,
y=\bar{Q}_{7}
). In any of these cases, by permuting
g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}
we obtain:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{4}\bigl({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}},\,x^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)+C_{4}\bigl(x^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)

(162)

\displaystyle+\,C_{4}\bigl(x^{\oplus},\,q_{5}^{\ominus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)+C_{4}\bigl(x^{\oplus},\,q_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\,C_{4}\bigl(x^{\oplus},\,q_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}}},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)\,.

Note, however, that care should be taken regarding the signs when permuting
g^{*}_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}
in CEVs (or in PEVs): an additional minus sign should be included whenever an odd permutation of quarks emerges. These are best illustrated using examples.
For
g^{*}g^{*}\rightarrow g\bar{q}q
, permuting
g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}
gives

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\ C_{3}\big(g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)-C_{3}\big(q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)+C_{3}\big(\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{3}\big(g_{4}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,g^{*}_{q_{1}^{\perp}},\,g^{*}_{q_{4}^{\perp}}\big).

(163)

Similarly, for the
g^{*}g^{*}\to x\,q\,\bar{q}\,y
example, permuting
g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}
, while adhering to our convention where
g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}
is the last entry in the colour-ordered CEV, introduces additional signs for odd permutations of fermions:

\displaystyle 0=\

\displaystyle C_{4}\bigl(g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}},\,x^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)+C_{4}\bigl(x^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,q_{6}^{\oplus},\,y^{\ominus},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{1}},\,g^{*}_{q^{\perp}_{5}}\bigr)
