Appendix A
Review of multi-parton kinematics

Here we collect our conventions for momenta and spinor products used throughout the paper. Following Appendix A in
DelDuca:1999iql
, we consider a
2\to n-2
process.
We choose a centre-of-mass frame where the two incoming particles, 1 and 2, are directly scattering at each other:

\displaystyle p_{1}=\left(\frac{p_{1}^{-}}{2},0,0,\frac{-p_{1}^{-}}{2}\right)\equiv\left(0,p_{1}^{-};0,0\right),

(195)

\displaystyle p_{2}=\left(\frac{p_{2}^{+}}{2},0,0,\frac{p_{2}^{+}}{2}\right)\equiv\left(p_{2}^{+},0;0,0\right)\,.

The remaining partons have non-zero transverse momenta, for which we will be using the complex momentum representation,
p_{\perp}=p^{x}+ip^{y}
. Thus, a four momentum vector is:

\displaystyle p_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\left(p_{i}^{+}+p_{i}^{-}\right)/2,\operatorname{Re}\big[{p_{i}^{\perp}}\big],\operatorname{Im}\big[{p_{i}^{\perp}}\big],\left(p_{i}^{+}-p_{i}^{-}\right)/2\right)

(196)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\big|{p_{i}^{\perp}}\big|\left(e^{y_{i}},e^{-y_{i}};\cos\phi_{i},\sin\phi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(p_{i}^{+},p_{i}^{-};{p}_{i}^{\perp}\big),

where each particle has a rapidity defined as

\displaystyle y_{i}=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{i}^{-}}\right),

(197)

and in the second line of eq. (
196
) the following on-shell condition is used

\displaystyle\big|{p_{i}^{\perp}}\big|^{2}=p_{i}^{+}p_{i}^{-}.

(198)

Momentum conservation yields

0=\sum_{i=3}^{n}{p_{i}^{\perp}},\qquad p_{2}^{+}=-\sum_{i=3}^{n}p_{i}^{+}\quad{\rm and}\quad p_{1}^{-}=-\sum_{i=3}^{n}p_{i}^{-}.

(199)

With this, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} s&=2p_{1}\cdot p_{2}=p_{1}^{-}p_{2}^{+}=\sum_{i,j=3}^{n}p_{i}^{+}p_{j}^{-},\\
s_{2i}&=2p_{2}\cdot p_{i}=p_{i}^{-}p_{2}^{+}=-\sum_{j=3}^{n}p_{i}^{-}p_{j}^{+},\\
s_{1i}&=2p_{1}\cdot p_{i}=p_{i}^{+}p_{1}^{-}=-\sum_{j=3}^{n}p_{i}^{+}p_{j}^{-},\\
s_{ik}&=2p_{i}\cdot p_{k}=p_{i}^{+}p_{k}^{-}+p_{i}^{-}p_{k}^{+}-{p_{i}^{\perp}}\bar{p}_{k}^{\perp}-{\bar{p}_{i}^{\perp}}p_{k}^{\perp}.\end{aligned}

(200)

Following Appendix A in
DelDuca:1999iql
, the Weyl spinor products are related to particle momenta by,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\langle p_{i}p_{j}\right\rangle={p_{i}^{\perp}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}-p_{j}^{\perp}\sqrt{\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}},\\
&\left\langle p_{2}p_{i}\right\rangle=-i\sqrt{\frac{-p_{2}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}{p_{i}^{\perp}}\text{, }\\
&\left\langle p_{i}p_{1}\right\rangle=i\sqrt{-p_{1}^{-}p_{i}^{+}}\text{, }\\
&\left\langle p_{2}p_{1}\right\rangle=-\sqrt{p_{2}^{+}p_{1}^{-}}.\\
&\end{aligned}

(201)

We finish this section by listing several identities used in this paper

\displaystyle[kp]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sign}\left(k^{0}p^{0}\right)\langle pk\rangle^{*},

(202)

\displaystyle\langle k|\not{q}|p]

\displaystyle=\langle kq\rangle[qp],

(203)

\displaystyle[p|\not{q}|k\rangle

\displaystyle=[pq]\langle qk\rangle,

(204)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}[ji]\langle ik\rangle

\displaystyle=0.

(205)

Appendix B
Factorization properties in various kinematic limits

B.1
High-energy limit

Within the high-energy factorization framework discussed in section
4.1
, when the rapidity separation between partons becomes large, scattering amplitudes can be decomposed into lower-point high-energy building blocks, such as MREVs
DelDuca:1998kx
;
DelDuca:1999iql
. The same methodology can also be applied starting from MREVs
DelDuca:1998cx
;
Fadin:1993wh
;
Fadin:1994fj
;
Fadin:1996yv
;
Byrne:2022wzk
;
Byrne:2023nqx
, and it serves as a crucial cross-check for our results.

In the appropriate large rapidity separation limit, say
y_{m}\gg y_{m+1}
(corresponding to
p_{m}^{+}\gg p_{m+1}^{+}
as in Eq. (
196
)), the CEV at leading order further factorizes into a product of lower-point CEVs and a
t
-channel (gluon) propagator pole,
1/|q_{m-2}^{\perp}|^{2}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-5.0pt\lim\limits_{y_{m}\gg y_{{m+1}}}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},p_{4}^{h_{4}},p_{5}^{h_{5}},\ldots,p_{m}^{h_{m}},p_{{m+1}}^{h_{{m+1}}},\ldots,p_{{n-1}}^{h_{{n-1}}},g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&=C_{m-3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},p_{4}^{h_{4}},\ldots,p_{m}^{h_{m}},g_{q_{m-2}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{|q_{m-2}^{\perp}|^{2}}\,C_{n-m-1}\big(g_{q_{m-2}^{\perp}}^{*},p_{{m+1}}^{h_{{m+1}}},\ldots,p_{{n-1}}^{h_{{n-1}}},g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{{m+1}}^{+}}{p_{m}^{+}}}\right).\end{split}

(206)

As an illustrative example, consider the colour-ordered CEV with a single gluon accompanied by a quark-antiquark pair emission. In the limit
y_{4}\gg y_{5}
, the factorization takes the form:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{y_{4}\gg y_{{5}}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=C_{1}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{2}^{\perp}}^{*})\frac{1}{t_{2}}C_{2}(g_{q_{2}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}).

(207)

That is, in MSLCV, when taking the limit
X_{2}\equiv\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}\to\infty
, and according to Eqs. (
88a
) to (
93
), the contributions from the second, third, and fifth terms in Eq. (
101
) factorize into Eqs. (
55
) and (
77
).
In Appendix
E.3
, we provide our corresponding checking code.

The factorization property holds in an analogous way for PEVs. Under the appropriate large rapidity separation condition, say
y_{m}\gg y_{{m+1}}
, a multi-particle PEV factorizes into a lower-point PEV and a CEV with
t
-channel gluon propagation between the large rapidity gap:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{y_{m}\gg y_{{m+1}}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to n-3}\big(p_{2}^{h_{2}},p_{3}^{h_{3}},\ldots p_{m}^{h_{m}},p_{{m+1}}^{h_{{m+1}}},\ldots,p_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}},g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1\to m-2}\big(p_{2}^{h_{2}},\ldots p_{m}^{h_{m}},g_{q_{m-2}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\frac{1}{|q_{{m-2}}^{\perp}|^{2}}C_{n-m-1}\big(g_{q_{m-2}^{\perp}}^{*},p_{{m+1}}^{h_{{m+1}}},\ldots,p_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}},g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big).

For example, a 4-parton-emission PEV under
{y_{5}\gg y_{6}}
factorizes as:

\lim_{y_{5}\gg y_{6}}\;P_{1\to 4}\bigl(q_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)=P_{1\to 3}\bigl(q_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)\frac{1}{\bigl|q_{3}^{\perp}\bigr|^{2}}C_{{1}}\bigl(g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{6}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr).

(209)

We used the code given in the Appendix
E.3
to verify that Eq. (
209
) holds with the MREVs in the library.
More high-energy limit factorization checks are provided in the
Examples.nb
file.
While our checks focus on the
X_{m-2}\equiv{p_{{m}}^{+}}/{p_{{m+1}}^{+}}\to\infty
limit, one may also
consider other high-energy limits, such as
X_{m-2}\to 0
. Detailed considerations for six-particle amplitudes can be found in Appendix G.1 of
Byrne:2022wzk
. Notably, the
O\bigl(\sqrt{p_{{m+1}}^{+}/p_{m}^{+}}\bigr)
corrections in Eq. (
206
) may encode a quark propagating across the large rapidity gap
Fadin:1976nw
;
Fadin:1977jr
, whose treatment requires a separate analysis beyond the scope of the present work.

B.2
Soft limit

The soft behavior of MREVs in QCD fundamentally originates from Weinberg’s soft theorem for scattering amplitudes
Weinberg:1965nx
. In the framework of colour-ordered parton amplitudes, when particle
i
corresponds to a soft gluon emission, the amplitude universally factorizes at leading order in the soft momentum into a lower-multiplicity amplitude multiplied by a
\mathcal{O}\left(1/p_{\text{soft }}\right)
soft factor, which captures the associated infrared singularity:

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}A_{2\to n-2}(1,2,3,4,\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\ldots,n-1,n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{2\to n-3}\bigl(1,2,3,4,\ldots,i-1,i+1,\ldots,n-1,n\bigr)\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},p_{i+1}\big)\,,

(210)

where
p_{i+1}
and
p_{i-1}
can be either gluons or (anti-)quarks while
g_{i}^{h_{i}}
is the soft gluon. The colour-ordered soft factor is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},g_{i}^{h_{i}},p_{i+1}\big)=\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{\varepsilon^{h_{i}}\bigl(p_{i}\bigr)\cdot p_{i-1}}{p_{i}\cdot p_{i-1}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{h_{i}}\bigl(p_{i}\bigr)\cdot p_{i+1}}{p_{i}\cdot p_{i+1}},

(211)

or in spinor-helicity variables:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},g_{i}^{\oplus},p_{i+1}\big)=\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{\langle(i-1)\,\,(i+1)\rangle}{\langle(i-1)\,i\rangle\,\langle i\,(i+1)\rangle},

(212a)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},g_{i}^{\ominus},p_{i+1}\big)=-\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\rightarrow 0}\frac{[\,(i-1)\,\,(i+1)\,]}{[\,(i-1)\,i\,][\,i\,(i+1)\,]}\,.

(212b)

Aside from the soft gluon itself, the soft factor is independent of both the helicities and the particle species of the colour-adjacent partons
{i-1}
and
{i+1}
appearing in Eqs. (
211
) and (
212
), and it is also invariant under rescaling of these momenta.

Having reviewed the soft factorization of colour-ordered amplitudes, our analysis will focus exclusively on the single-gluon soft limits of colour-ordered MREVs. In contrast to the case of scattering amplitudes, the position of the soft gluon within the colour ordering of a PEV or CEV induces two distinct factorization patterns:

•

Central soft limit:
Occurs when the soft gluon
i
, together with its colour-adjacent hard partons
(i\pm 1)
, lie within the on-shell emission sequence of the PEV or CEV.

•

Edge Soft Limit:
Arises when the soft gluon
i
is colour-adjacent to an off-shell gluon.

In the following, we elaborate on these two cases in turn.

Central soft limit

Let us derive the factorization properties of a CEV in the soft limit from Eq. (
210
).
To this end we consider the two
relevant limits in different orders: on the one hand the soft limit of the emitted gluon labelled
i
(with
4<i<n-1
, satisfying centrality), and on the other the high-energy limit needed to extract the CEV from the amplitude, as in Eq. (
24
).
By applying the high-energy limit first we obtain:

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\to 0}\left(\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\cdots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg\,y_{n}}\;A_{2\to n-2}\big(1,\,2,\,3,\,4,\,\ldots,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,n\big)\right)

\displaystyle=s\,P_{1\to 1}\big(2,\,3,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}\,P_{1\to 1}\big(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\to 0}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big),

(213)

and by applying the soft limit first we get:

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\cdots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg\,y_{n}}\;\left(\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\to 0}A_{2\to n-2}\big(1,\,2,\,3,\,4,\,\ldots,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,n\big)\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{y_{3}\gg y_{4}\,\simeq\,\cdots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg\,y_{n}}\;A_{2\to n-3}\big(2,\,3,\,4,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 90.0pt\times\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,p_{i+1}\big)

\displaystyle=s\,P_{1\to 1}\big(2,\,3,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}\,P_{1\to 1}\big(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle\quad\times C_{n-5}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,p_{i+1}\big)

(214)

Comparing Eqs. (
213
) and (
214
) and stripping off the common factors

s\,P_{1\to 1}\big(2,\,3,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}\,P_{1\to 1}\big(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,n,\,1\big)

we are left with

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{i}^{\mu}\to 0}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=C_{n-5}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,p_{i+1}\big).

(215)

Therefore, as long as colour-adjacent neighbours of the soft gluon remain within the emission sequence, the CEV factorizes in the same way as the amplitude, and similarly for the PEV:

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{i}\rightarrow 0}

\displaystyle\,P_{1\to n-3}\big(2,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\,P_{1\to n-4}\big(2,\,\ldots,\,i-1,\,i+1,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{i-1},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{i}^{h_{i}}},\,p_{i+1}\big).

(216)

As a concrete demonstration, consider the soft gluon with

p_{5}^{\mu}\rightarrow\lambda\,p_{5}^{\mu}=(\lambda p_{5}^{+},\,\lambda p_{5}^{-};\,\lambda p_{5}^{\perp})

(217)

where we scale
\lambda\to 0
. In this limit, the soft factor
\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{4},g_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}\big)\sim\frac{1}{\lambda}
, as shown in Eq. (
211
). Implementing the MSLCV parameterization from Eq. (
10
), this corresponds to the following scaling laws:

\displaystyle X_{2}\to\frac{X_{2}}{\lambda},\ z_{2}\to\frac{z_{2}}{\lambda},\ X_{3}\to{\lambda X_{3}}.

(218)

Considering for example the colour-ordered three-gluon CEV
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
of Eq. (
96
), using the parameterization of Eq. (
218
), one can verify that the CEV factorizes into a two-gluon CEV given in Eq. (
61
),
multiplied by a soft factor as follows:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{p_{5}^{\mu}\to\lambda p_{5}^{\mu}}C_{3}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{5}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)=\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{4},g_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}\right)C_{2}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\right),

(219)

where in MSLCV

\displaystyle\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{4},g_{5}^{\oplus},p_{6}\right)=\lim\limits_{{p_{5}^{\mu}\to\lambda p_{5}^{\mu}}}\frac{\langle 46\rangle}{\langle 45\rangle\langle 56\rangle}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\frac{\left(z_{1}-1\right)z_{2}^{2}\left(X_{2}X_{3}z_{1}-z_{3}+1\right)}{z_{1}\left(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}\right)\left(X_{3}z_{2}-z_{3}+1\right)q_{1}^{\perp}}\,.

(220)

Note the homogeneous scaling of Eq. (
220
) as
1/\lambda
subject to the scaling law, Eq. (
218
).
The corresponding code that checks this factorization property is given in Appendix
E.3
.

Edge soft limit

There are situations which the central soft limit does not cover. These correspond to the edge soft limit. Still working with Eq. (
210
), we can consider the case where gluon 4 becomes soft, i.e.
p_{4}^{\mu}\to 0
. Then, under the rapidity ordering
y_{3}\gg y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}
, we can derive the edge soft limit factorization for the CEV by applying the soft and high-energy limit in different orders.
To begin with, consider applying first the high-energy limit which defines the CEV, and then the soft limit:

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\lim_{p_{4}^{\mu}\to 0}\left(\lim_{y_{3}\gg y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}}A_{2\to n-2}\big(2,\,3,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n,\,1\big)\right)

\displaystyle=s\,P_{1\to 1}\big(2,\,3,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}\,P_{1\to 1}\big(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\lim_{p_{4}^{\mu}\to 0}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,.

(221)

Next, consider taking the soft limit first, and then the high-energy limit:

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\gg y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}}\lim_{p_{4}^{\mu}\to 0}A_{2\to n-2}\big(2,\,3,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{y_{3}\gg y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}}A_{2\to n-3}\big(2,\,3,\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,n,\,1\big)\,\operatorname{Soft}\big(3,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5\big)

\displaystyle=s\,P_{1\to 1}\big(2,\,3,\,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\frac{1}{t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}\,P_{1\to 1}\big(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},\,n,\,1\big)

\displaystyle\quad\times C_{n-5}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\operatorname{Soft}\big(+,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5\big),

(222)

where we define

\operatorname{Soft}\left(+,g_{4}^{h_{4}},p_{{5}}\right)\equiv\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,y_{5}}\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{{3}},g_{4}^{h_{4}},p_{{5}}\right)\,.

(223)

Here we used the inherent rescaling invariance of the soft factor to manifest its independence on
p_{3}
.
Combining Eqs. (
222
) and (
221
), we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{4}^{\mu}\to 0}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=C_{n-5}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-1,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\,\operatorname{Soft}\big(+,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5\big).

(224)

Let us now take a closer look at
\operatorname{Soft}\big(+,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{4}^{h_{4}}},\,5\big)
.
Following Eq. (
201
) we find that, in general, for the edge soft factor with
p_{x}^{+}\gg p_{i}^{+}\sim\,p_{j}^{+}
,

\operatorname{Soft}\left(+,g_{i}^{\oplus},p_{j}\right)=\lim_{p_{x}^{+}\gg\,p_{i}^{+}\sim\,p_{j}^{+}}\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{x},g_{i}^{\oplus},p_{j}\right)=\lim_{p_{x}^{+}\gg\,p_{i}^{+}\sim\,p_{j}^{+}}\frac{\langle xj\rangle}{\langle xi\rangle\langle ij\rangle}=\frac{p_{j}^{\perp}}{p_{i}^{\perp}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}}\frac{1}{\langle ij\rangle}.

(225)

Following similar analysis,

\operatorname{Soft}\left(+,g_{i}^{\ominus},p_{j}\right)=\frac{\bar{p}_{j}^{\perp}}{\bar{p}_{i}^{\perp}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{i}^{+}}{p_{j}^{+}}}\frac{1}{[ij]}.

(226)

We have thus seen that despite the soft gluon being adjacent to the virtual gluon, the factorization of the CEV in this limit is consistent: the kinematic dependence on the right-hand side of Eq. (
224
) involves only the variables the CEV depends upon.

A similar conclusion holds of course when the soft gluon is colour-adjacent to the off-shell gluon that connects to negative rapidities:

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{n-1}^{\mu}\to 0}C_{n-4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-2,\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}}},\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=C_{n-5}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,4,\,5,\,\ldots,\,n-2,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\;\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{n-2},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}g_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}}},\,-\big).

(227)

Here, the edge soft factor
\operatorname{Soft}(p_{n-2},\,g_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}},\,-)
is defined analogously to Eq. (
225
). Specifically, from Eq. (
201
), when
p_{i}^{+}\sim p_{j}^{+}\gg p_{y}^{+}
, we have

\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{j},\,g_{i}^{\oplus},\,-\right)\equiv\lim_{p_{i}^{+}\sim p_{j}^{+}\gg\,p_{y}^{+}}\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{j},\,g_{i}^{\oplus},\,p_{y}\right)=\lim_{p_{i}^{+}\sim p_{j}^{+}\gg\,p_{y}^{+}}\frac{\left\langle j\,{y}\right\rangle}{\left\langle j\,i\right\rangle\left\langle i\,{y}\right\rangle}=\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}\,\frac{1}{\langle j\,i\rangle}.

(228)

Following similar analysis,

\operatorname{Soft}\left(p_{j},\,g_{i}^{\ominus},\,-\right)=\sqrt{\frac{p_{j}^{+}}{p_{i}^{+}}}\frac{1}{[ji]}.

(229)

This completes our discussion of the CEV edge soft limit. We find that the PEV likewise admits the same factorization in the edge soft limit.

\displaystyle\lim_{p_{n-1}^{\mu}\to 0}P_{1\to n-3}\Big(2,\,3,\,\ldots,\,n-2,\,g_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}},\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=P_{1\to n-4}\Big(2,\,3,\,\ldots,\,n-2,\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\Big)\;\operatorname{Soft}\big(p_{n-2},\,g_{n-1}^{h_{n-1}},\,-\big).

(230)

The soft limit factorization examples above and several others can be found in the MREV package file
Examples.nb
.
To close the soft limit section, we note that the soft limit of real parton emission has been systematically extended beyond single-gluon to two-parton
Catani:1999ss
, three-parton
DelDuca:2022noh
;
Catani:2022hkb
and most recently four-parton
Chen:2024hvp
soft emission currents. Such currents can also be obtained from the CEVs and PEVs, however, we leave this for future work.

B.3
Collinear Limit

The collinear behaviour of MREVs in QCD originates from the collinear factorization of scattering amplitudes. For colour-ordered parton amplitudes, collinear singularities emerge exclusively when
colour-adjacent
partons become collinear, as described by the factorization formula:

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0ptA_{2\to n-2}\bigl(2,3,4,\ldots,i,i+1,\ldots,n-1,n,1\bigr)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{i\|i+1}

\displaystyle\sum_{h_{c}=\pm}A_{2\to n-3}\bigl(2,3,4,\ldots,P^{h_{c}},\ldots,n-1,n,1\bigr)\times\,\text{Sp}_{P^{-h_{c}}}\bigl(x_{i}P^{h_{i}},\,x_{{i+1}}P^{h_{{i+1}}}\bigr),

(231)

where the collinear momentum
P=p_{i}+p_{i+1}
is parameterized as
p_{i}=x_{i}P,p_{i+1}=x_{i+1}P
, with momentum fractions satisfying
x_{i}+x_{i+1}=1
.

Considering two colour-adjacent partons
i
and
j
, the collinear splitting amplitudes, which exhibit
1/\sqrt{s_{ij}}
behaviour as
s_{ij}=(p_{i}+p_{j})^{2}\to 0
, are given by
DelDuca:1999iql
;
Bern:1994zx
;
Mangano:1990by
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\operatorname{Sp}_{g^{\ominus}}\left(g_{i}^{\oplus},g_{j}^{\oplus}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{x_{i}x_{j}}\langle ij\rangle}&\hskip 30.0pt\operatorname{Sp}_{g^{\oplus}}\left(g_{i}^{\oplus},g_{j}^{\ominus}\right)=\frac{x_{j}^{2}}{\sqrt{x_{i}x_{j}}\langle ij\rangle}\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{g^{\oplus}}\left(\bar{q}_{i}^{\ominus},q_{j}^{\oplus}\right)=\frac{-x_{i}}{\langle ij\rangle}&\hskip 30.0pt\operatorname{Sp}_{g^{\oplus}}\left(\bar{q}_{i}^{\oplus},q_{j}^{\ominus}\right)=\frac{x_{j}}{\langle ij\rangle}\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{\bar{q}^{\ominus}}\left(q_{i}^{\oplus},g_{j}^{\oplus}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{x_{j}}\langle ij\rangle}&\hskip 30.0pt\operatorname{Sp}_{q^{\oplus}}\left(\bar{q}_{i}^{\ominus},g_{j}^{\oplus}\right)=\frac{x_{i}}{\sqrt{x_{j}}\langle ij\rangle}\\
\operatorname{Sp}_{\bar{q}^{\ominus}}\left(g_{i}^{\oplus},q_{j}^{\oplus}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{x_{i}}\langle ij\rangle}&\hskip 30.0pt\operatorname{Sp}_{q^{\oplus}}\left(g_{i}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{j}^{\ominus}\right)=\frac{x_{j}}{\sqrt{x_{i}}\langle ij\rangle}.\end{array}

Other helicity configurations can be obtained through complex conjugation of these expressions in Eq. (
B.3
).

Equipped with this, let us now consider the collinear limit of MREVs. Consider first the CEV case. Taking the high-energy limit of Eq. (
231
) as in Eq. (
24
), we have:

\displaystyle\hskip-25.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}}A_{2\to n-2}\bigl(2,3,4,\ldots,i,i+1,\ldots,n-1,n,1\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.0pt=\,s\,P_{1\to 1}\left(2,3,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}P_{1\to 1}\left(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},n,1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 65.0pt\times C_{n-4}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},4,\ldots,i,i+1,\ldots,n-1,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\,.

(236)

Taking first the collinear limit
i||(i+1)
and then the high-energy limit we find:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip 1.0pt\lim_{y_{3}\gg\,y_{4}\,\simeq\,\ldots\,\simeq\,y_{n-1}\gg y_{n}}A_{2\to n-2}\bigl(2,3,4,\ldots,i,i+1,\ldots,n-1,n,1\bigr)\\
&\xrightarrow{i\|i+1}s\,P_{1\to 1}\left(2,3,g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\frac{1}{t_{1}}\frac{1}{t_{n-3}}P_{1\to 1}\left(g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*},n,1\right)\\
&\hskip 40.0pt\times\sum_{h_{c}=\pm}C_{n-5}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},4,\ldots,P^{h_{c}},\ldots,n-1,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\,\text{Sp}_{P^{-h_{c}}}\bigl(x_{i}P^{h_{i}},\,x_{{i+1}}P^{h_{{i+1}}}\bigr).\end{split}

(237)

Thus, from Eqs. (
B.3
) and (
237
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&C_{n-4}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},4,\ldots,i,i+1,\ldots,n-1,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\\
&\hskip 40.0pt\xrightarrow{i\|i+1}\sum_{h_{c}=\pm}C_{n-5}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},4,\ldots,P^{h_{c}},\ldots,n-1,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\,\text{Sp}_{P^{-h_{c}}}\bigl(x_{i}P^{h_{i}},\,x_{{i+1}}P^{h_{{i+1}}}\bigr).\end{split}

(238)

The collinear behaviour for PEVs follows similarly

\displaystyle\hskip-40.0ptP_{1\to n-3}\bigl(2,\,,\ldots,i,\,{i+1},\ldots,{n-1},\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\xrightarrow{i\|i+1}

\displaystyle\sum_{h_{c}=\pm}\,P_{1\to n-4}\bigl(2,\ldots,P^{h_{c}},\ldots,{n-1},\,g_{q_{n-3}^{\perp}}^{*}\bigr)\,\,\text{Sp}_{P^{-h_{c}}}\bigl(x_{i}P^{h_{i}},\,x_{{i+1}}P^{h_{{i+1}}}\bigr).

(239)

Next, we present an explicit example illustrating the collinear limit of a four-parton CEV.

Example: collinear limit of a four-parton CEV

We analyze the collinear limit
p_{5}\parallel p_{6}
in the CEV
C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
.
Our first goal is to derive the MSLCV behaviour in the limit. The momenta
p_{5}
and
p_{6}
merge along a shared direction
P
, parameterized as

p_{5}\to x_{5}P,\qquad p_{6}\to x_{6}P,\qquad\text{ with }x_{5}+x_{6}=1.

(240)

We should manifest the splitting directly in the MSLCVs. Firstly, the ratio of momenta amongst the collinear pair in Eq. (
240
) directly fixes the MSLCV
X_{3}
, defined in Eq. (
10
) as the ratio of plus lightcone momenta:

Figure 19:

The MSLCVs for 8-point kinematics and their reduction via collinear factorization to 7-point kinematics in the collinear limit
p_{5}\parallel p_{6}
, with
p_{5}\to x_{5}P
and
p_{6}\to x_{6}P
. In terms of MSLCV, this corresponds in the 8-point case to
z_{3}\to 1+z_{2}{x_{5}}/{x_{6}}
and
X_{3}\to{x_{5}}/{x_{6}}
. The resulting 7-point kinematics, in terms of the new MSLCV, are related to those of the original 8-point configuration as
\{z_{2}^{\prime}=({z_{2}+x_{6}})/{x_{5}},\,X_{2}^{\prime}=X_{2}x_{5},\,{X_{4}^{\prime}={X_{4}}/{x_{6}}}\}
.

X_{3}=\frac{p_{5}^{+}}{p_{6}^{+}}\to\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}.

(241)

Secondly, from the MSLCV definition given in Eq. (
10
), one can first derive

\frac{q_{3}^{\perp}}{q_{4}^{\perp}}=-\frac{z_{3}}{1-z_{3}}

(242)

using momentum conservation. Then from Eq. (
242
), using the definitions of
z_{2}
and
z_{3}
, we obtain:

\left.\begin{aligned} &z_{2}=-\frac{q_{3}^{\perp}}{p_{5}^{\perp}}=-\frac{q_{3}^{\perp}}{x_{5}P^{\perp}}\\
&z_{3}=-\frac{q_{4}^{\perp}}{p_{6}^{\perp}}=-\frac{q_{4}^{\perp}}{x_{6}P^{\perp}}\end{aligned}\right\}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\frac{z_{2}}{z_{3}}=-\frac{x_{6}(1-z_{3})}{x_{5}z_{3}}.

(243)

Solving Eq. (
243
) yields
z_{3}\to 1+z_{2}{x_{5}}/{x_{6}}
, which also holds for the conjugate variable
\bar{z}_{3}
. Consequently, in the MSLCVs, the collinear limit corresponds to

\displaystyle\left\{z_{3}\to\frac{x_{5}z_{2}+x_{6}}{x_{6}},\bar{z}_{3}\to\frac{x_{5}\bar{z}_{2}+x_{6}}{x_{6}},X_{3}\to\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}\right\}.

(244)

Consider now the spinor products corresponding to the collinear pair (which can be determined in terms of the MSLCVs using Eqs. (
201
) and (
10
)):

\langle 56\rangle=\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}(1+X_{3}z_{2}-z_{3})}}{\sqrt{X_{3}}(z_{2}-1)(z_{3}-1)}\qquad[56]=\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}(1+X_{3}\bar{z}_{2}-\bar{z}_{3})}}{\sqrt{X_{3}}(\bar{z}_{2}-1)(\bar{z}_{3}-1)}\,.

(245)

We observe that
in the collinear limit, Eq. (
244
),
both spinor products
\langle 56\rangle
and
[56]
simultaneously vanish.
Thus, in the CEV
C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
terms containing the
1/\langle 56\rangle
and
1/[56]
factors emerge as the dominant divergences in the collinear limit
p_{5}\parallel p_{6}
.
Upon substituting Eq. (
244
) into the MSLCV representation of this CEV, one indeed obtains the expected factorization of Eq. (
238
).
Our next task is to determine how the lower-point CEV depends on the collinear parameters
x_{4}
,
x_{5}
, and the original lightcone variables MSLCVs, i.e.
X_{2}
,
X_{4}
, and
z_{2}
.
Figure
19
, illustrates the transition from the original four-parton CEV
C_{4}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
to the 3-parton CEV
C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{P}^{h},g_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}\big).

This corresponds to the mapping:

\{X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},q_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}\}\quad\longrightarrow\quad\{X_{2}^{\prime},X_{4}^{\prime},q_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},z_{2}^{\prime},z_{4}\},

(246)

where the primed variables corresponding to
C_{3}
. Their definition according to (
10
) is:

\displaystyle X_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{P^{+}},

\displaystyle X_{4}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{P^{+}}{p_{7}^{+}},

\displaystyle z_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=-\frac{q_{4}^{\perp}}{P^{\perp}}.

The relation defining the collinear map (
246
) are:

\displaystyle\text{Transverse:}\quad z_{2}^{\prime}=-\frac{q_{4}^{\perp}}{P^{\perp}}=-x_{5}\frac{q_{3}^{\perp}-p_{6}^{\perp}}{p_{5}^{\perp}}=x_{5}\left(z_{2}+\frac{x_{6}}{x_{5}}\right);

(247a)

\displaystyle\text{Longitudinal:}\quad X_{2}^{\prime}=\frac{p_{4}^{+}}{P^{+}}=\frac{x_{5}p_{4}^{+}}{p_{5}^{+}}=X_{2}x_{5},\quad\quad X_{4}^{\prime}=\frac{P^{+}}{p_{7}^{+}}=\frac{p_{6}^{+}}{x_{6}p_{7}^{+}}=\frac{X_{4}}{x_{6}}\,.

(247b)

This leads to the complete transformation rules:

\left\{z_{2}^{\prime}={z_{2}x_{5}+x_{6}},\ X_{2}^{\prime}=X_{2}x_{5},\ X_{4}^{\prime}=\frac{X_{4}}{x_{6}}\right\}.

(248)

In the collinear limit
p_{5}\parallel p_{6}
, (
244
), the function

C_{4}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,g_{4}^{\oplus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{7}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*})

has simple poles at
z_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}z_{2}
and
\bar{z}_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}\bar{z}_{2}
, whose residues are, respectively,

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt\underset{z_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}z_{2}}{\text{Res}}C_{4}(g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\ominus})(X_{2},\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}},X_{4},q_{1}^{\perp},\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2},z_{3},\bar{z}_{3},z_{4},\bar{z}_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}(g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{P}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\ominus})\big(X_{2}^{\prime},X_{4}^{\prime},q_{1}^{\perp},\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1},z_{2}^{\prime},\bar{z}_{2}^{\prime},z_{4},\bar{z}_{4}\big)\underset{z_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}z_{2}}{\text{Res}}\operatorname{Sp}_{g_{-P}^{\oplus}}\left(x_{5}q_{P}^{\oplus},x_{6}q_{P}^{\ominus}\right),

and

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0pt\underset{\bar{z}_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}\bar{z}_{2}}{\text{Res}}C_{4}(g_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{7}^{\oplus})(X_{2},\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}},X_{4},q_{1}^{\perp},\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2},z_{3},\bar{z}_{3},z_{4},\bar{z}_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}(g_{4}^{\ominus},g_{P}^{\oplus},g_{7}^{\oplus})\left(X_{2}^{\prime},X_{4}^{\prime},q_{1}^{\perp},\bar{q}_{1}^{\perp},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1},z_{2}^{\prime},\bar{z}_{2}^{\prime},z_{4},\bar{z}_{4}\right)\underset{\bar{z}_{3}=1+\frac{x_{5}}{x_{6}}\bar{z}_{2}}{\text{Res}}\operatorname{Sp}_{g_{-P}^{\ominus}}\left(x_{5}q_{P}^{\oplus},x_{6}q_{P}^{\ominus}\right),

with the primed variable substitution according to (
248
).
These correspond to the two terms with helicities plus and minus in (
238
).
The code implementing this check is given in the Mathematica file
Examples.nb
.

Appendix C
Brief introduction to the Kleiss-Kuijf relation

In this appendix we will illustrate how the Kleiss-Kuijf relation can be derived for pure gluon amplitudes. We then comment on its generalization to amplitudes containing one or two quark-antiquark pairs. In section
7.2
we then use these relations to derive corresponding ones for MREVs.
For a comprehensive discussion and justification of the general-multiplicity case, we refer the reader to Refs.
DelDuca:1999rs
;
Weinzierl:2016bus
.

One way to obtain the Kleiss-Kuijf relation between colour-ordered amplitudes is to compare between the trace basis and the DDM
DelDuca:1999rs
basis for a colour-dressed amplitude. Let us illustrate this using the six-point colour-dressed pure gluon amplitude
\mathcal{A}(1,2,3,4,5,6)
. This amplitude can be expressed in either the trace basis or the DDM basis, respectively, as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(1,2,3,4,5,6)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{5}}\tr(T^{a_1}T^{a_{\sigma_2}}T^{a_{\sigma_3}}T^{a_{\sigma_4}}T^{a_{\sigma_5}}T^{a_{\sigma_6}})A(1,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},\sigma_{5},\sigma_{6})

(251)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{4}}F^{a_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}}x_{1}}F^{x_{1}a_{\sigma_{3}}x_{2}}F^{x_{2}a_{\sigma_{4}}x_{3}}F^{x_{3}a_{\sigma_{5}}a_{6}}A(1,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},\sigma_{5},6),

(252)

where for brevity we denote each particle by
i\equiv g_{i}^{h_{i}}
.
Using the six-point version of the relation (
36
), e.g.

\displaystyle F^{a_{1}a_{2}x_{1}}F^{x_{1}a_{3}x_{2}}F^{x_{2}a_{4}x_{3}}F^{x_{3}a_{5}a_{6}}=\operatorname{tr}\left(T^{a_{1}}\left[T^{a_{2}},\left[T^{a_{3}},\left[T^{a_{4}},\left[T^{a_{5}},T^{a_{6}}\right]\right]\right]\right]\right)\,,

(253)

we find that when going from the DDM basis expression in (
252
) to the trace basis in (
251
), the DDM basis elements contribute to the specific trace
\operatorname{tr}\left(T^{a_{1}}T^{a_{2}}T^{a_{3}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{4}}\right)
as

\displaystyle(F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{3}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,2,3,4,5,6)+(F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{3}}F^{a_{5}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,2,4,3,5,6)

\displaystyle+(F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{3}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,2,4,5,3,6)+(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{3}}F^{a_{5}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,4,2,3,5,6)

\displaystyle+(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{3}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,4,2,5,3,6)+(F^{a_{4}}F^{a_{5}}F^{a_{2}}F^{a_{3}})_{a_{1}a_{6}}A(1,4,5,2,3,6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \operatorname{tr}\left(T^{a_{1}}T^{a_{2}}T^{a_{3}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{4}}\right)\Big(A(1,2,3,4,5,6)+A(1,2,4,3,5,6)+A(1,2,4,5,3,6)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt+A(1,4,2,3,5,6)+A(1,4,2,5,3,6)+A(1,4,5,2,3,6)\Big)+\ldots

(254)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \operatorname{tr}\left(T^{a_{1}}T^{a_{2}}T^{a_{3}}T^{a_{6}}T^{a_{5}}T^{a_{4}}\right)A(1,2,3,6,5,4)+\ldots

(255)

where the ellipsis stand for other elements in the trace basis according to Eq. (
253
).
Since the trace basis elements are linearly independent, it follows from
Eqs. (
254
) and (
255
), that

\displaystyle A(1,2,3,6,5,4)=\sum_{\shuffle}A(1,\{2,3\}\shuffle\{4,5\},6),

(256)

where
\shuffle
means the shuffle product.
From this perspective, the
Kleiss-Kuijf relation is a
consistency condition between the DDM basis decomposition in Eq. (
252
) and the trace basis one in Eq. (
251
).
One similarly has

\displaystyle A(1,2,3,7,6,5,4)=-\sum_{\shuffle}A(1,\{2,3\}\shuffle\{4,5,6\},7)

(257)

where the additional minus sign originates from the extra layers of nested commutators in Eq. (
253
).

Working with fermions in the adjoint representation, e.g. in the context of
{\cal N}=4
sYM,
the result easily generalises to fermions, where one simply needs to account for the extra sign arising from odd permutations of fermions.
Then, since the colour-ordered amplitudes do not depend on the colour representation, these relations carry over to QCD.
For example,

\displaystyle A(1,q_{2},{\bar{q}}_{3},6,5,4)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\shuffle}A\big(1,\{q_{2},{\bar{q}}_{3}\}\shuffle\{4,5\},6\big),

(258)

\displaystyle A(1,2,3,6,{\bar{q}}_{5},q_{4})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\shuffle}A\big(1,\{2,3\}\shuffle\{q_{4},{\bar{q}}_{5}\},6\big),

(259)

and a more interesting one:

\displaystyle A(1,Q_{2},{\bar{Q}}_{3},6,{\bar{q}}_{5},q_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\shuffle}(-1)^{w(\shuffle)}A\big(1,\{Q_{2},{\bar{Q}}_{3}\}\shuffle\{q_{4},{\bar{q}}_{5}\},6\big),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(A(1,Q_{2},{\bar{Q}}_{3},q_{4},{\bar{q}}_{5},6)-A(1,Q_{2},q_{4},{\bar{Q}}_{3},{\bar{q}}_{5},6)+A(1,Q_{2},q_{4},{\bar{q}}_{5},{\bar{Q}}_{3},6)

\displaystyle\qquad+A(1,q_{4},Q_{2},{\bar{Q}}_{3},{\bar{q}}_{5},6)-A(1,q_{4},Q_{2},{\bar{q}}_{5},{\bar{Q}}_{3},6)+A(1,q_{4},{\bar{q}}_{5},Q_{2},{\bar{Q}}_{3},6)\Big),

(260)

where the sign of each term is fixed by
w(\shuffle)
, which equals
1
and
0
for fermion permutations that are odd and even, respectively, relative to the reference ordering
\{Q_{2},\bar{Q}_{3},q_{4},\bar{q}_{5}\}
.

Appendix D
Colour-dressed PEVs with three emissions

D.1
PEV
g^{*}g\rightarrow g\bar{q}q

The PEV for
gg^{*}\rightarrow g\bar{q}q
was first computed in Ref.
DelDuca:1999iql
. Here, we review the colour dressing and present the full PEV for
\mathcal{P}_{1\to 3}^{g\to g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},\,g_{3}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},\,q_{5}^{\oplus},\,g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
in MSLCV form.

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1\to 3}^{g\to g\bar{q}q}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

(261)

\displaystyle g^{3}\Big[\big(T^{c_{3}}T^{d_{2}}T^{d_{3}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(T^{d_{2}}T^{c_{3}}T^{d_{3}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(T^{d_{3}}T^{c_{3}}T^{d_{2}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(T^{d_{3}}T^{d_{2}}T^{c_{3}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(T^{d_{2}}T^{d_{3}}T^{c_{3}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(T^{c_{3}}T^{d_{3}}T^{d_{2}}\big)_{i_{5}\bar{\imath}_{4}}P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)\Big]\,,

where the colour-ordered PEVs entering (
261
) are:

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle-\frac{X_{1}X_{2}^{5/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big)z_{1}z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{1}-z_{1}+1\big)\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle\frac{X_{2}^{3/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big){}^{2}\big(z_{2}-1\big)z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{1}-z_{1}+1\big)\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\frac{X_{2}^{5/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big){}^{2}z_{1}\big(z_{2}-1\big)z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big)\big(X_{1}X_{2}z_{1}-(1-z_{1})(1-z_{2})\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt-\frac{X_{1}X_{2}^{5/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big)z_{1}\big(z_{2}-1\big)z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big)\big(X_{1}X_{2}z_{1}-(1-z_{1})(1-z_{2})\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle\frac{X_{2}^{3/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big)\big(z_{2}-1\big)z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\big(g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)=

\displaystyle\frac{X_{2}^{5/2}\big(z_{1}-1\big)z_{1}z_{2}}{\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big){}^{2}\big(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\big){q_{1}^{\perp}}^{2}}\,.

(262)

The pole
\big(X_{1}X_{2}+X_{2}+1\big)
is always positive in the physical region, as it corresponds to ratios of plus light-cone components, as shown in Eq. (
10
).
The other poles in Eq. (
262
) are collinear poles. They correspond to the following spinor brackets:

\displaystyle\left\langle 34\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left(X_{1}-z_{1}+1\right)}\,q_{1}^{\perp}}{\sqrt{X_{1}}\,(z_{1}-1)}

(263a)

\displaystyle\left\langle 35\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left(X_{1}X_{2}z_{1}-(1-z_{1})(1-z_{2})\right)}\,q_{1}^{\perp}}{\sqrt{X_{1}}\,\sqrt{X_{2}}\,(z_{1}-1)\,(z_{2}-1)}

(263b)

\displaystyle\left\langle 45\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left(X_{2}z_{1}-z_{2}+1\right)}\,q_{1}^{\perp}}{\sqrt{X_{2}}\,(z_{1}-1)\,(z_{2}-1)}\,.

(263c)

As in Eq. (
10
) (see also section
B.3
) these poles in Eq. (
263c
) can be reached within the physical region by varying the transverse momenta to
z_{i}=1
.

Next we show how the individual colour-ordered PEVs in Eq. (
261
) are obtained from
MREV

yuyu_2017_github
. One first
loads the data in terms of MSLCVs

⬇

Import
[
"PEV_1to3.m"
],

and initialises the momentum representation via

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevtopevp[\#]\&/@}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\big\{\mathtt{pev[P,P,qM,qP]},&\mathtt{pev[P,qPc,qMc,Pc]},&\mathtt{pev[P,qP,qM,P]}\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 5.69046pt\mathtt{pev[P,Pc,qPc,qMc]},&\mathtt{pev[P,P,qPc,qMc]},&\mathtt{pev[P,Pc,qM,qP]}\big\},\end{array}\,.

The code conventions are introduced in section
6
. The momentum representations for the colour-ordered PEVs in Eq. (
261
) can then be obtained using the following command:

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,P,qM,qP][3,4,5]}

(264)

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(g_{3}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,qPc,qMc,Pc][5,4,3]}

(265)

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,qP,qM,P][4,5,3]}

(266)

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,Pc,qPc,qMc][3,5,4]}

(267)

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,P,qPc,qMc][3,5,4]}

(268)

\displaystyle P_{1\to 3}\left(g_{3}^{\oplus},g_{2}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{pevp[P,qM,qP,Pc][4,5,3]}.

(269)

D.2
PEV
\bar{q}g^{*}\to q\bar{Q}Q

Here we consider PEVs with two quark-antiquark pairs. The momentum representation for the full PEV for
\mathcal{P}_{1\to 3}^{\bar{q}\to q\bar{Q}Q}\big(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},\,q^{\oplus}_{3},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},\,Q^{\oplus}_{5},\,g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
was presented in
DelDuca:1999iql
. We show how to obtain this result using our
MREV

yuyu_2017_github
library.

The colour dressing of the PEV
\mathcal{P}_{1\to 3}^{\bar{q}\to q\bar{Q}Q}\big(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},\,q^{\oplus}_{3},\,\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},\,{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},\,g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\hskip-20.0pt\mathcal{P}_{1\to 3}^{\bar{q}\to q\bar{Q}Q}\left(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)=\\
&g^{3}\left[T_{j_{5}\bar{\imath}_{2}}^{c}\delta_{i_{3}\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P\left(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}T_{i_{3}\bar{\imath}_{2}}^{c}\delta_{j_{5}\bar{\jmath}_{4}}P\left(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\right.\\
&\left.+T_{i_{3}\bar{\jmath}_{4}}^{c}\delta_{j_{5}\bar{\imath}_{2}}P\left(\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}T_{j_{5}\bar{\jmath}_{4}}^{c}\delta_{i_{3}\bar{\imath}_{2}}P\left(\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)\right]\\
&-\delta_{qQ}(1\leftrightarrow 3)\text{. }\\
&\end{aligned}

(270)

We first load the PEVs, written in MSLCVs, using:

⬇

Import
[
"PEV_1to3.m"
],

and then initialise the momentum representation as

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevtopevp[\#]\&/@}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\big\{\mathtt{pev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}]},&\mathtt{pev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,q\}]},\\[2.84526pt]
\hskip 5.69046pt\mathtt{pev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}]},&\mathtt{pev[\{qM,q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,Q\}]}\big\}\,.\end{array}

Then, the colour-ordered PEV appearing in (
270
) in the momentum representation are given by

\displaystyle P\left(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,Q\}][3,4,5]}

\displaystyle P\left(\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qM,Q\},\{qP,q\}][5,4,3]}

\displaystyle P\left(\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,q\},\{qPc,Q\},\{qMc,Q\}][3,5,4]}

\displaystyle P\left(\bar{Q}^{\ominus}_{4},{q}^{\oplus}_{3},\bar{q}^{\ominus}_{2},{Q}^{\oplus}_{5},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathtt{pevp[\{qM,q\},\{qP,Q\},\{qPc,q\},\{qMc,Q\}][5,3,4]}.

(271)

Appendix E
Examples of
MREV
library of PEVs and CEVs

In this section we provide additional information regarding the use of our
MREV
library
yuyu_2017_github
, supplementing its introduction in section
6
. An important goal of this appendix is to demonstrate some of the checks we performed. We begin with a brief explanation of the initialization process of the package and a summary of frequently-used functions. We then provide examples verifying the factorization properties of the obtained MREVs in high-energy and soft limits. We also provide examples of the photon decoupling identity to demonstrate some of the checks we performed for the colour-ordered MREVs results collected in the library. For brevity, we do not display here examples for collinear factorization, the Kleiss-Kuijf identity, the reversal identity, or the supersymmetric Ward identity. For interested readers, we provide several examples of each of these relations in the accompanying notebook
Examples.nb
in
MREV
library.

E.1
Initialising

One can use the following command to initialise the package:

⬇

In
[1]:=

SetDirectory
[
"path/to/MREVinit.m"
];

%
set

directory
,

please

modify

the

path
,

In
[2]:=

Import
[
"MREVinit.m"
];

%
initialise

the

package
.

E.3
Checking factorization

In this section, we present explicit examples for checking the high-energy and soft factorization of both types of MREVs.

High-energy limit factorization of a three-emission CEV

In this section, we check the following factorization property of the quark-antiquark gluon CEV

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{X_{2}\to\infty}C_{3}\left(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\right)=C_{1}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},g_{4}^{\oplus},g_{q_{2}^{\perp}}^{*})\frac{1}{t_{2}}C_{2}(g_{q_{2}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{5}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}).

(272)

High-energy limit factorization of a four-emission CEV

Here we consider the factorization of a multiplicity four CEV in the high-energy limit. While feasible, this check is computationally intensive to perform analytically. For practical purposes, a numerical check is perfectly adequate, and we demonstrate it in what follows.

We first define the numerical high-energy limit target function,
NMRKcheck8N
, in which
X2_
represents an arbitrary longitudinal momentum ratio. It is important to note that the tree-level propagator
t
has already been factored out within the definition of this function.

⬇

In
[5]:=

NMRKcheck8N
[
x_
,

n_
,

X2_
,

t_
]

:=

x

*(
t

//

getMinimalgn
[#,

n
]

&)

//

ReplaceAll
[
X2

->

2^32].

Next we check the factorization of
C_{4}
in the high-energy limit

\displaystyle\lim_{X_{3}\to\infty}C_{4}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*})=C_{2}(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\bar{q}_{4}^{\ominus},q_{5}^{\oplus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*})\frac{1}{t_{3}}C_{2}(g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*},q_{6}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{7}^{\ominus},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}).

(273)

To this end we write the left-hand side and right-hand side as follows:

⬇

=

,

,

,

][
(*@$

\texttt{q}[1,‘‘\perp*"],

\texttt{z}_1,

\texttt{zb}_1,

\texttt{z}_2,

\texttt{zb}_2,

\texttt{z}_3,

\texttt{zb}_3,

$@*)

\displaystyle\text{nptx}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &x_{2}=1.2,\quad x_{3}=1.32,\quad x_{4}=1.4,\\

&z_{1}=1.386+1.16829\,\mathrm{i},\quad\bar{z}_{1}=1.386-1.16829\,\mathrm{i},\\
&z_{2}=1.21346+1.11293\,\mathrm{i},\quad\bar{z}_{2}=1.21346-1.11293\,\mathrm{i},\\

High-energy limit factorization of a four-emission PEV

In this section, we check

\displaystyle\lim_{X_{3}\to\infty}P_{1\to 4}(q_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{6}^{\oplus},g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*})=P_{1\to 3}(q_{2}^{\oplus},g_{3}^{\ominus},g_{4}^{\oplus},\bar{q}_{5}^{\ominus},g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*})\frac{1}{t_{3}}C_{2}(g_{q_{3}^{\perp}}^{*},g^{\oplus}_{6},g_{q_{5}^{\perp}}^{*}).

Soft Limit of a CEV

To verify the soft factorization described in Eq. (
219
), we first prepare the function to be checked

⬇

checkfn

=

cev
[
P
,

P
,

M
];

(*input

the

CEV

needed

to

be

checked*)
,

and the soft factor corresponding to the positive-helicity gluon,

⬇

qcw

=

ab
[4,

6]/(
ab
[4,

5]

ab
[5,

6])

//

getMinimalgn
[#,

7]

&

//

Factor
;

soft

=

qcw

/.

(*@$\texttt{X}_4

->

\texttt{X}_1$@*)

//

Map
[
Series
[#,

{
(*@$\texttt{X}_1$@*)
,

\[
Infinity
],

0}]

&]

//

Normal
;

(*input

the

soft

factor*)
.

We prepare the left-hand side (LHS) by taking the soft limit of the original CEV,

\lim_{k_{5}^{\mu}\to\lambda k_{5}^{\mu}}C_{3}\big(g_{q_{1}^{\perp}}^{*},\,q_{4}^{\oplus},\,g_{5}^{\oplus},\,\bar{q}_{6}^{\ominus},\,g_{q_{4}^{\perp}}^{*}\big)
,
and dividing it by the corresponding soft factor,

\operatorname{Soft}(p_{4},\,g_{5}^{\oplus},\,p_{6})

⬇

=

cevc
[

,

P
,

M
][
(*@$

\texttt{X}_2,

\texttt{X}_3,

\texttt{z}_1,

\texttt{zb}_1,

\texttt{z}_2,

\texttt{zb}_2,

\texttt{z}_3,

\texttt{zb}_3

$@*)
]

=

(

[#,

]

&

/
@

/.

{
(*@$

\texttt{X}_2

\texttt{X}_2/\lambda,

\texttt{zb}_2/\lambda,

->

\lambda

\texttt{X}_3$@*)
}

//

[

[#,

{
(*@$\lambda$@*)
,

//

;
